Cash and bank transfers are written down as owed back: both balances show them until the instructor who taught the lesson, or an owner or manager, marks them handed back from the learner card, which cannot be undone. A fee on a lesson nobody had paid is owed in the balance from the moment the lesson was called off, shown as a late cancellation fee, and can be marked paid in cash or by bank transfer for the amount of the fee. Paying a fee by card, on screen or from a saved card, comes with no-show fees in M3-19. Only a lesson that is on (confirmed or under way) can be cancelled late: withdrawing a request or dropping a slot held for a card costs nothing however close to the time it is. The cancellation event carries the policy it was cancelled under (the window, the percentage and how many minutes before the start) and what became of the money, and the learner's notification, the same in the inbox, as a push and by email, explains a kept fee from those facts. The instructor and the school are told what happened, without the policy.
- **Options:** Refund a card payment in full and charge the fee again; leave cash and transfers for the instructor to give back and record by hand; keep treating a request withdrawn close to the time as a late cancellation.
- **Reason:** Keeping the fee out of what was paid is one partial refund, or nothing at all when the fee is the whole price (acceptance test 4). Refunding and charging again is two card movements for one fee, fees on both, and a second chance for the bank to ask the learner to confirm. Software cannot hand back cash, but it can record that it is owed, so the learner and the Business see the same debt until somebody says it is settled, which is also what the money dashboard needs (MNY-01). The instructor who taught the lesson may mark it handed back because they usually hold the cash; this is not issuing a refund, which PRD 6.2 keeps to the people who run the Business, because the amount and the reason were already decided by the cancellation (R-08). A request withdrawn late was already recorded as late in M2, which cost nothing while fees were never collected; now that an unpaid fee is owed, it would bill learners for lessons that never happened. The explanation is built from what the event recorded rather than worked out again later, so a Business changing its policy afterwards cannot change why a fee was kept.

## D-093 | 2026-09-14 | A no-show settles like a late cancellation, and a fee nothing paid goes to the kept card
- **Decision:** Marking a lesson as a no-show settles its money exactly as the learner cancelling late does (D-092): the fee its Business's policy sets is kept out of the credit that paid for it or out of the money already paid, and the rest goes back. The lesson records `dispute_until`, seven days from when it was marked, and the event carries it (R-09). A fee nothing has paid, for a lesson called off late or nobody came to, is charged by a job to the newest working card the learner keeps with the Business, with nobody at the keyboard. Only at a Business that takes cards and does not have its learners pay in person, and only for a learner it keeps a card for. When there is no card the fee is simply owed, since the learner was already told so. A card that is refused, has run out or needs its holder to confirm marks the fee failed, tells both sides once, and sends the learner to pay it on screen. A fee can be paid on the pay screen like a lesson, with nothing held, or in person for the fee's amount. A card payment that arrives for a lesson owing a fee pays the fee, and anything over it goes back. The "Save this card" box, and the note for a lesson charged the day before, now say the Business can charge a late cancellation or no-show fee to the card under its cancellation policy. A no-show is its own notification kind, `booking.no_show`, sent to the people a cancellation goes to on the same channels, because PRD Appendix B has no row for it.
- **Options:** Charge the fee only after the seven days to dispute are over; charge a card whenever the learner has ever paid by card, even at a Business that takes payment in person; say nothing about fees when a card is kept, since PAY-09 already says fees go to saved cards; send a no-show as a cancellation notification.
- **Reason:** R-09 treats a no-show as a late cancellation, so one set of money rules keeps the warning, the balance and the notification consistent. Charging straight away is how a late cancellation already works: the fee is kept at once from anything paid. If a dispute is upheld, giving the money back is one refund, and a fee left uncharged for a week is often never collected. Charging a card with nobody present needs the cardholder's agreement to that kind of charge under the card schemes' rules for stored cards (the same reason as D-079), so the box has to say it. A Business that chose in-person payment has not asked its learners for cards. The consent wording is plain English, not legal advice, and belongs in the solicitor's review of payment terms that PRD 15 requires before launch, together with D-086. "Lesson cancelled" would be the wrong title for a lesson nobody came to, and a notification kind is cheap.

## D-094 | 2026-09-14 | A learner disputes a no-show from their lessons, and an owner or manager decides
- **Decision:** For the seven days in `dispute_until`, a learner marked as a no-show can dispute it once from their own lessons, saying what happened in up to 1,000 characters. The dispute is a row of its own (`no_show_disputes`). The learner who raised it, the owners and managers, the instructor whose lesson it was and platform staff can read it, and nobody can write it except through the functions. The instructor and the owners and managers are told, and the learner card lists the learner's disputes with what they said. An owner or manager (the people who can refund, D-091) decides: waiving the fee gives back whatever paid it, the way it came (credit to the lots it was kept from as an adjustment with who and why, card money through the refund job, cash owed back), and leaves nothing owed or to charge. Keeping it changes nothing. They can add a note, which the learner sees with the lesson, and the learner is told either way. What the learner and the Business write stays in the database and never goes into an event. A lesson nobody came to now says "No-show" rather than "Cancelled" in the diary and the learner's lessons.
- **Options:** Let the instructor who marked the no-show decide; give disputes to platform support, as PRD 6.3 lists them for Support Admin; take a dispute by email.
- **Reason:** R-09 gives the learner the right to dispute but does not say who decides. Waiving a fee is giving money back, and PRD 6.2 keeps that for the people who run the Business. For a sole trader that is the same person, and in a school it is not the instructor being disputed. Support handling disputes platform-wide is ADM-08, a P2 feature; until it exists the Business decides its own, which is also who took the money. Putting the dispute next to the balance on the learner card means the person deciding sees what is owed as they decide. A row per dispute, rather than columns on the lesson, keeps what the learner wrote away from the many places that read lessons, and gives support something to list when ADM-08 comes.

## D-095 | 2026-09-14 | Receipts are numbered per Business, frozen when issued, and wait out the undo window for cash
- **Decision:** Every payment a learner makes gets a receipt, issued by a job when the payment is received and emailed to the learner once. Numbers run 1, 2, 3 for each Business from a counter only the issuing function touches, so no number is skipped or used twice. A receipt keeps what it said on the day it was issued: the Business's name, address and VAT number, what was paid for (a lesson with its date, length and instructor, a late cancellation or no-show fee, or credit), the amount and how it was paid. A later refund is shown on the receipt page and does not change the receipt. VAT appears only when the Business has a VAT number: the VAT included in the price, at 20%, to the nearest penny. Money recorded in person waits ten minutes, the time it can still be taken back out (D-089), so a receipt is never issued for a payment that is then removed. The owner sets the address and VAT number on the Money screen. The receipt page at `/receipts/{payment}` can be read and printed by the learner, whoever paid, the people who run the Business and the learner's instructor, and each payment in the history links to its receipt once it has one.
- **Options:** Number receipts across the whole platform; work VAT out again whenever a receipt is shown; issue cash receipts at once and void them if the payment is taken back; send receipts through the general notification email.
- **Reason:** A Business's receipts are its own records, so the numbers should run in its own series. A receipt that changed when the Business later registered for VAT or moved would no longer say what was said at the time. Waiting ten minutes costs nothing and means no receipt is ever issued for money that was taken back out. A receipt needs its own layout, with lines, VAT and a total, which the notification template does not have. VAT on driving lessons is at the standard rate, and PAY-08 shows it only for registered Businesses. Whether a receipt can also serve as a full VAT invoice, which needs the customer's details for sales over £250, goes to the accountant review in PRD 15.

## D-096 | 2026-09-14 | What the money dashboard counts, and for whom
- **Decision:** The Money screen opens with "Money in" for this week (Monday to Sunday), this month or this tax year (6 April to 5 April), with each period's dates worked out in London and turned into instants only at the edges. Paid is money received for lessons and fees in the period, by card, cash and bank transfer. Credit sold is packages bought, in money and in hours. Refunds is money that went back, to cards or in person. Credit given back as credit is not money, so it is not counted. Unpaid is lessons in the period that have started and are not paid, plus late cancellation and no-show fees still owed. Lessons still to come are not counted. One function, `money_summary`, reads all of it and checks who is asking. The owners and managers see the whole Business. An instructor who does not run it sees only their own lessons, with no credit sold, since a package belongs to the Business rather than to a lesson.
- **Options:** Count a lesson as paid in the period it was taught rather than when the money arrived; show school instructors the whole school; count unpaid as everything owed at the moment, whatever period is chosen.
- **Reason:** Money arriving is what a tax year is about for someone who is self-employed and on the cash basis, which most instructors are, and it matches the receipts. PRD 6.2 keeps a school's totals from its instructors, and ARCHITECTURE 8.6 says school instructors see their own earnings unless the school allows more; that setting is MNY-06, P2. Unpaid follows the chosen period so the figures on one card all describe the same days. What is owed overall is on each learner's balance.

## D-097 | 2026-09-14 | The instructor on a lesson sees who it is with, and only people who may book a learner can put them in a diary
- **Decision:** An instructor reads the `users` row (name and contact details) of every learner they have a lesson with, whatever became of the lesson, for as long as their membership is active. That is what a cover lesson needs: when the office books one of Tom's learners with Emma, the learner stays Tom's (LRN-06) and Emma's diary says who the lesson is with (DIA-03, BOK-01). Nothing else comes with the lesson. The learner's date of birth and licence number, the notes kept about them, their learner profile, saved pickup points, age band, balance, history, and their place on the learner list and card all stay with the people who teach them and run the Business. Because a lesson now reveals its learner, `create_booking` and `book_weekly` check who may book before anything is written: an instructor books the learners assigned to them, and somebody who manages bookings (owners, managers, or an instructor the school gives that permission) books any learner the Business has. A learner can still book a lesson with any instructor themselves. A lesson whose learner the diary cannot read shows as "Unnamed learner" instead of taking the diary down.
- **Options:** Widen `auth_can_see_learner` to anybody with a lesson; reassign the learner to whoever covers; read diary names through a function that returns the name and nothing else; leave the booking checks as they were.
- **Reason:** PRD 6 gives a school instructor their own diary and their assigned learners. A lesson in their own diary is theirs to teach, and nobody can teach a lesson without knowing who it is with, so this follows the lesson rather than the assignment. `auth_can_see_learner` also decides pickup points (writing them too), the age band and the learner history, so widening it would hand a covering instructor what their colleague keeps; reassigning would move the learner's notes, history and balance for the sake of one lesson. A name-only function would leave out the phone number a covering instructor needs on the day, and add a second way of reading lessons beside row-level security; the `users` row is what staff who work with a learner already read (ARCHITECTURE 6.3). The policy matches `bookings_select_instructor`, so an instructor can see a lesson exactly when they can see its learner. The booking checks are what make it safe. `create_booking` let an instructor book any learner the Business had, and `book_weekly` wrote its weekly plan before checking anything, which the nightly sweep then booked with nobody asking (tested: a plan for somebody the school had never taught became four lessons). With lessons revealing learners, either would let an instructor read the details of anyone whose id they had, including a learner they had been moved away from. PRD 6.2 already limits a school instructor to their own learners, so the check enforces the permission matrix rather than adding a rule. Three database tests had Ian, a school instructor, book Ivy's learner Lou; they now book as the manager or for Ian's own learner, with the same assertions. Not covered here: no screen attaches a pickup point to a lesson yet, and when one does, `create_booking` should check the point belongs to the learner before a lesson's instructor can read it. Weekly plans made before this change are not checked again, and reassigning a learner still leaves their weekly slot with the instructor who had them.

## D-098 | 2026-09-14 | Who hears about money, and when
- **Decision:** A payment received is told to the learner in the app and on their phone, but not by email, because the receipt is their email (D-095). The instructor is told on every channel: the lesson's instructor, or for credit the learner's own instructor. Whoever marked money paid in person is not told about it. Nobody is told about cash until its ten-minute undo window has passed (D-089). A school's owners and managers are not told about each payment. Instead they get one summary at 08:00 each morning, covering the day before from London midnight to midnight, and nothing for a day with no payments. The summary can be switched off; the other money notifications are service messages. A lesson or fee still owed two days after it was due is told once to the learner, the instructor and the school. When it was due follows the lesson's terms, the same way the balance works it out (D-090). The check runs at 10:00 each morning and looks back 30 days. For a lesson booked to be paid in person, the learner is not chased; the instructor is asked to mark it paid if it was. When using credit leaves 2 hours or less, having left more before, the learner and their instructor are told, once for each package bought.
- **Options:** Send the school summary in the evening for that day; tell owners and managers about every payment; chase an overdue lesson every day until it is paid; tell about low credit each time a lesson uses it below the line; email the learner about each payment as well as sending the receipt.
- **Reason:** A summary sent in the evening would leave out payments made later that day, and no summary would ever include them; a morning summary of the whole day before misses nothing. Appendix B gives schools a daily summary rather than a message for each payment, and gives learners their receipt. Telling each person once, at a sensible hour, stops a reminder turning into nagging, and the balance still shows the lesson as overdue (PAY-06). When a pay-in-person lesson looks unpaid, the likeliest reason is cash nobody marked as paid, so asking the learner to pay again would be wrong. The 30-day limit stops old debts all being announced at once when this goes live. Telling an instructor about cash they marked as paid themselves is noise.

## D-099 | 2026-09-14 | Card details go into the provider's own fields, dressed as ours
- **Decision:** A card typed in on the pay screen, the package screen or the save card screen goes into Stripe's Payment Element. Stripe draws those fields in its own frame, and they are styled with our input tokens. Nothing about a card reaches our servers. When something goes wrong, the screen says so in our own words, chosen by Stripe's error code. Stripe's own sentence about saving the card is switched off, because our screens already say what a kept card can be charged for. When a bank wants to check it is the card holder before charging a kept card, the screen asks the learner there and then, rather than sending them to type the card in again. The frame uses the system font, because it cannot load the app's copy of Inter. Only ways to pay that happen on the screen are offered: cards, Apple Pay, Google Pay and Link. Methods that send the learner to another site to pay, such as Klarna, Pay by Bank and Revolut Pay, are left out.
- **Options:** Stripe Checkout, a hosted page away from the app; the older Card Element, which takes cards only; showing Stripe's error messages and its terms sentence as they come; offering every method the Business's account has switched on, redirects included.
- **Reason:** The Payment Element keeps the learner on our screen, runs the bank's check (strong customer authentication), and shows Apple Pay and Google Pay where they are set up, as ARCHITECTURE 8.1 plans. Stripe's messages are written for any shop and can change without notice, while our copy rules (plain words, no dashes) have to hold on every screen. Stripe's sentence would repeat the consent our screens already ask for, in different words. That consent wording is already going to the solicitor review (PRD 15). A learner sent to another site comes back to a screen that cannot yet tell a payment on its way from no payment, and could pay twice; the PRD asks for cards and wallets, which never leave the page.

## D-100 | 2026-09-15 | A lesson record is saved once from any device, by the instructor who taught the lesson
- **Decision:** A lesson record's id is made on the device. `save_lesson_record` saves it once: the same id sent again changes nothing and says it was already saved. A different id for a lesson that already has a record is refused with ALREADY_RECORDED, which the app shows as a conflict. Only the instructor who taught the lesson writes its record; the owner and manager of a school read records but do not write them, as PRD 6.2 has it. A record needs at least one skill rated from 1 to 5, each skill once, and a line about the lesson. The focus for next time, homework and how long the form took are optional. A lesson that has started but is not yet marked done is marked done by saving its record, with the same audit row and event as marking it done. A lesson that has not started, or was called off, gets no record. Records are read by the learner, with every Business they learn with; by the owners and managers of the Business that wrote them; by an instructor, for their own records and for every record of a learner assigned to them there; and by platform staff. `visibility` has two values. `learner` is what every record gets in Phase 1. `business` keeps a record from the learner, is not yet set by any screen, and is what the Driving Passport's sharing rules (PRG-04) will build on. A lesson with a record cannot be deleted.
- **Options:** Let the owner or manager write records; let a record be saved again to change it; require the lesson to be marked done before a record can be saved; give instructors every record in their Business.
- **Reason:** With no signal, the phone cannot know whether a record it sent arrived, so sending it again must be harmless, and two phones must not both write one. With no signal, marking the lesson done and saving its record both happen on the phone, so they have to arrive together; refusing the record because the lesson was not yet marked done would lose it. PRD 6.2 gives school instructors their own learners' progress, not the whole school's. A record is the learner's, so deleting the lesson under it would take their progress with it. Changing a saved record is not in M4, so a save only ever creates one.

## D-101 | 2026-09-15 | Today lists the day's lessons, and a lesson's timer, taps and record are kept on the phone until saved
- **Decision:** Today now shows the day's lessons as PRD 7.5 describes: the time, the learner, the pickup point and where the money stands, with Navigate to the pickup and a big Start lesson button for the lesson under way or next. The M1 plan only covered Today's setup checklist, and M4-10 needs a Today with lessons to take offline. Start lesson opens the lesson: a timer from when it was opened, the learner's name, and the 23 skill areas to tap. Finish lesson turns it into the record: the tapped skills rated 1 to 5 first, with the full list behind a button, then a line about the lesson, the focus for next time and optional homework. The timer, taps and words are kept in the browser's storage under the lesson, with the record's id made when the draft starts, so a reload or a closed tab loses nothing. The record goes to `/api/v1/lesson-records`, a route rather than a Server Action, because M4-11's service worker can only call a route. The route takes JSON from this app's own origin only. Done in the diary marks the lesson done and opens its record, as PRD 10.2 step 3 has it. A lesson that has already ended opens straight on its record.
- **Options:** Start the timer on the server by marking the lesson in progress; save the record through a Server Action and add a route later for the outbox; keep the taps only in the page's memory.
- **Reason:** A timer and taps that need the server stop working the moment the signal does, which is exactly where lessons happen (PRG-09). One route for saving with signal and for sending later means one path to test and no second behaviour to drift. Browser storage is enough for a draft on one phone; M4-11 moves the finished record into the offline outbox.

## D-102 | 2026-09-15 | Progress goes by when lessons happened, and the skill map shows each area's latest rating
- **Decision:** Each lesson record keeps the time its lesson started (`lesson_starts_at`, set by `save_lesson_record` from the booking). The learner's timeline is in that order, newest lesson first, and so is the skill map's idea of latest. When a record arrives does not change either. An area on the skill map shows the rating from the latest lesson that rated it, not an average, together with how many lessons have rated it. The roll-up is the `skill_progress` view: at most one row for each area a learner has been rated in. The view runs as the caller, so each reader's map is built from the records they may read. For the learner, that is every Business they learn with. For a Business, it is its own records. Every one of the 23 areas is shown in test report order, including areas not started yet. The line above the map says how many areas have been worked on, and how many are driven independently when there are any.
- **Options:** Order by when the record was saved; average an area's ratings, or take its best; send every rating to the page and roll them up there; show only the areas worked on.
- **Reason:** A record written with no signal can arrive days after a newer one (PRG-09), so ordering by arrival would put an old lesson on top and let an old rating overwrite a newer one. The rating scale measures how much help the learner needed that day, so the latest lesson says where they are now. An average would still count the first lesson against them months later, and a best would hide a skill that has slipped. Keeping the lesson time on the record means readers don't need the booking behind it, and a school instructor reading a colleague's record for their learner can't always see that booking. Rolling up in the database keeps a learner rated hundreds of times well inside the 1,000 rows the API returns at once. Showing all 23 areas makes it just as clear what is left as what is done, the way the test report will mark it.

## D-103 | 2026-09-15 | The progress screen: records and skill map side by side, or under two tabs on a phone
- **Decision:** The learner's Progress tab shows lesson records and the skill map side by side on a wide screen. On a phone they sit under two tabs, with records first, because the newest record is what a learner opens the screen for after a lesson (PRD 10.3). Records come 10 at a time. Show older records adds the next page under the ones already shown, from `GET /api/v1/lesson-records?learner=&before=`. The cursor is the last record's lesson time and id, so records at the same moment neither repeat nor go missing. The route gives whoever asks only the records they may read, so a learner they may not see comes back with no records. A record card shows when the lesson was and who with (plus the school, for a lesson at a school), what the instructor wrote, each skill rated, and the focus for next time and homework when there are any. A lesson's year is shown only when it is not this year. An instructor gets the same screen for a learner of theirs, one tap from the learner card, which now carries a Progress summary: the skill map line and the last record. The skill bar takes its labels from core, so the rating scale is written in one place.
- **Options:** Put the map and the records on one long page at every width; number the pages and load each one as a new page; put the instructor's view on the learner card itself; show the Business's name on every record.
- **Reason:** On a phone, a map of 23 areas above the records would push the newest record 1,200 pixels down. Adding older records under the ones already shown keeps the reader's place, where a new page would send them back to the top past the map. A route, rather than a Server Action, is what the future native app can call too (PRD 8.1). The learner card is already long, and progress is one tap away through the summary. An independent instructor's Business is the instructor, so their name alone says who taught the lesson.

## D-104 | 2026-09-15 | The app installs from brand.ts, and keeps Today and a lesson's screen as they open
- **Decision:** The web app manifest is built from brand.ts: the name, tagline and colours, a standalone display, and `/start` as the page it opens on. `/start` sends somebody signed in straight to their portal. The icons are drawn from the brand's colours when the app is built: 192 and 512 pixels, a 512 pixel one Android may crop to its own shape, and a 180 pixel home screen icon for iPhones. The service worker is registered on every portal page, after the page has loaded. It keeps a copy of Today and of a lesson's own screen each time one opens with signal: the network first, waiting up to four seconds, then the copy. It also keeps the build's files those screens are drawn with. Those files are named by their contents, so a kept copy is used first. The first page a device ever opens arrives before the worker does, so the page asks the worker to keep it and its files once the worker is running. Any other screen needs the network, and with none it gets a plain page that says so, with a Try again link. Nothing from the build is precached. Whenever the sign in page opens, after signing out or when a session has ended, the kept copies of screens are deleted from the device. The install offer is a card on Today and on the learner's home. Where the browser hands over its install prompt, the card has an Install button. On an iPhone or iPad it gives the Share and Add to Home Screen steps in words instead. Nothing is offered once the app is installed or after Not now on that device. Lighthouse dropped its installable check in version 12, and the full Chromium that answers Chrome's own check will not run on the development machine. So the Playwright test checks each thing Chrome looks for: the manifest as Chrome itself read it, a name, a standalone display, a start page that opens, and icons whose PNG headers match their stated sizes.
- **Options:** Precache the whole build, about 5 MB, when the worker installs; precache Today when the worker installs; use Serwist's default caching, which keeps every page and API answer anybody opens; let the browser show its own install bar; ship icon files in `public/`.
- **Reason:** Today and a lesson's screen are all PRD 8.1 asks to work with no signal. Those screens hold a person's lessons, so they can only be kept once that person opens them. Keeping every page and API answer would store learners' details on devices for no purpose. That is also why the copies go when somebody reaches sign in: a shared or handed-on device keeps nobody's diary. Sending 5 MB of every portal's code to a learner who never needs it offline costs their data plan for nothing. A plain HTML page, rather than an app page, still works with nothing else on the device. It also keeps the address of the screen that failed, so Try again reloads that screen. Safari on iOS has no install prompt, so the steps in words are the only way to offer it there. Drawing the icons from brand.ts keeps the brand in one file.

## D-105 | 2026-09-15 | The phone keeps today's and tomorrow's lessons whole, and clears them at sign in
- **Decision:** The instructor portal keeps today's and tomorrow's lessons in IndexedDB, through Dexie. It reads them from `GET /api/v1/instructor/lessons` when the portal opens, when the signal comes back, and when the app is looked at again, at most once a minute. The route answers with whose lessons they are, which days in London the answer covers, and when it read them. A fresh answer replaces the kept copy whole, in one transaction. A read that fails, is refused, or happens with no signal leaves the last good copy where it was. So that a failed read can never pass for an empty day, the query behind Today now raises its error instead of returning no lessons. Each kept lesson carries what Today and a lesson's screen show: the learner's name, the pickup, where the money stands, and whether it has a record. The skill areas are part of the app's code, so they need no keeping. The copy records which days it covers, so a day it never read is unknown rather than empty. It is deleted, with the kept screens, whenever the sign in page opens. The screens themselves are not fetched ahead: M4-10 draws Today and a lesson from this copy when there is no signal.
- **Options:** Keep the lessons in the service worker's cache as API answers; merge each read into the kept copy lesson by lesson; fetch the screen of every lesson today and tomorrow ahead of time, so each opens from the cache; keep a week.
- **Reason:** Replacing the copy whole means a lesson moved away or called off is gone from the phone as it is from the diary, with no merge rules to get wrong. An API answer in a cache cannot be asked for one day's lessons, and its time of reading is harder to show. Fetching every lesson's screen ahead would render up to ten pages each time an instructor opens the app, for screens the kept lessons can draw themselves. Today and tomorrow is what PRD 8.1 asks for, and a week would hold learners' details on the phone for no one's use.

## D-106 | 2026-09-15 | With no signal, Today draws itself from the phone's copy, and a lesson opens on a screen made for it
- **Decision:** With no signal, a yellow banner across the instructor portal says so, and says Today and its lessons still open. Today's list switches to the phone's copy of the day (D-105) whenever that copy covers the day it is on the phone. It works out the next lesson, and the lessons that need their record, from the phone's clock, and a line says when the lessons were read. Without such a copy it keeps the lessons the screen came with, still by the phone's clock. Its lesson buttons become plain links, so a tap opens a whole page the service worker can answer. A lesson whose own screen was kept opens from that copy. Any other lesson goes to `/app/instructor/lessons/offline?lesson=<id>`, a screen kept whenever Today opens, which draws the lesson from the phone's copy with the same timer and record form. With signal back, it hands over to the lesson's own screen, and the draft carries across because it is kept under the lesson. The installed app starts on `/start`, which with no signal sends somebody to Today when Today is kept. When the worker keeps a screen, it also keeps every build file that screen's HTML names, so a screen that has never been drawn on the device still runs. In development, React's debug information is sent inside the page rather than over the dev server's WebSocket (`experimental.reactDebugChannel: false`). Otherwise a kept screen waits for that socket before it comes alive, so the app in development would never work offline as the built one does.
- **Options:** Open a lesson over Today in a sheet with no change of address; fetch every lesson's own screen ahead of time (D-105); keep Today's links as app navigation and rely on the app falling back to a whole page; read the lesson from the app's search params; test offline behaviour against a production build only.
- **Reason:** A real address for a lesson keeps the back button, a reload, and handing over to the lesson's own screen working the same with and without signal. An app navigation with no signal depends on how the framework handles a failed request, while a plain link always reaches the service worker. The kept screen's copy was taken without a lesson in its address, and the app's search params describe the copy, so the lesson is read from the address the phone shows. A kept page from hours ago, or yesterday, shows the wrong next lesson unless the phone's clock decides. Running the offline tests on the development server as well as in CI means a failure shows up before it is pushed, which is what the debug channel setting allows. That setting changes nothing in the built app.

## D-107 | 2026-09-15 | A lesson record goes into an outbox on the phone before it is sent
- **Decision:** Saving a lesson record puts it in an outbox on the phone (IndexedDB, database `lesson-outbox`) under the person who saved it, and only then sends it. Sent, or already saved by an earlier try, it leaves the outbox and the screen says it is saved. If the server says the lesson already has a record from another phone, or turns the record down for another reason, it leaves the outbox and the form says why, with the draft still there. With no signal it stays and Today says it is waiting. On browsers with Background Sync, the browser is asked to wake the service worker once the signal is back. On every browser, the instructor portal sends waiting records when it opens, when the signal returns and when the app is looked at again, before it re-reads today and tomorrow. A record that turns out to be a conflict or a refusal at that point shows on Today, with the lesson, the reason and Dismiss, until somebody reads it. One sender runs at a time on a device, through the Web Locks API. A saved record waits for a sender already running rather than leaving its fate unknown. Records are only sent while the phone's kept lessons belong to the person who saved them. The outbox is not cleared at sign in. With no signal, saving goes back to Today as a whole page, so the service worker can answer it, and Today's notice says the record is waiting.
- **Options:** Send first and keep the record only when sending fails; let Serwist's Background Sync plugin queue the failed requests; send every waiting record whoever is signed in; clear the outbox at sign in with everything else kept for no signal.
- **Reason:** A record kept before it is sent survives the app closing half way through sending, which is exactly when signal is patchy. The server saves one record per id however often it arrives (D-100), so sending from a page and the service worker, or twice, is safe. Serwist's queue replays requests blind: it could not tell the instructor that another phone had already recorded the lesson, and it hides whether a record is still on the phone. iPhones have no Background Sync, so the portal itself has to send when it can. A record sent under somebody else's session would be refused as not their lesson and lost. So it waits for the person who saved it, and clearing it at sign in would throw away a lesson's record somebody taught and wrote.

## D-108 | 2026-09-15 | A city is a group of local authority districts, and its areas are those districts
- **Decision:** The public pages' cities are groups of local authority districts, named as the Office for National Statistics names them. postcodes.io returns that name for every postcode, and the postcode cache keeps it (`admin_district`). The launch cities are the three the plan names: Leeds, Manchester (Greater Manchester's ten boroughs) and London (its 32 boroughs and the City of London). They live in `public.cities` and `public.city_districts`, written by migrations only; the first launch region is the product owner's decision (PRD 20), and a city is added with a migration. In a city of several districts, each district is an area with its own page, as `/driving-lessons/london/croydon`, except the district the city is named after, whose page is the city's. A postcode outside every launch city is a place of its own: its district under the name people say ("Bristol, City of" is Bristol, "Glasgow City" is Glasgow), with no city page above it, so every instructor has a profile address. `place_of_postcode` answers from the cache. The naming rules are in `packages/core/src/places.ts` and in the database, and a test on each side pins them to the same names.
- **Options:** Group postcode districts ("LS6") into named neighbourhoods by hand; use electoral wards; leave instructors outside the launch cities without a city in their address; keep the list of cities in the app's code.
- **Reason:** The district comes with every postcode lookup already, so nothing has to be mapped by hand and every name is the official one rather than a guess. Neighbourhoods by district would be hundreds of hand-typed rows with nothing to check them against, and wards split familiar places in two (Didsbury East and Didsbury West). The PRD's own example area, Croydon, is a borough. Cities in the database let city pages, sitemaps and profile queries join on them. Leeds is a single district, so it has no area pages until a finer division is chosen.

## D-109 | 2026-09-15 | Public pages live on doovor.com, and the app sends them there
- **Decision:** The public pages of PRD 8.3, starting with instructor profiles, are served on the public site's host, `doovor.com`. The same path opened on `app.doovor.com` moves there for good. Links from those pages into the app, such as Book a lesson and each free time, use the app's address. A preview deployment or this machine serves both from one host. Canonical addresses use the site's address (`getSiteUrl`), and each public page adds its prefix to `isSitePath` in `lib/hosts.ts` when it arrives.
- **Options:** Keep public profiles on the app's host until the marketplace opens, as D-084 expected; serve them on both hosts, with canonical tags to say which one counts.
- **Reason:** These pages exist to be found by search (PRD 14.6), and search wants each page at one address. D-084 put what is meant to be searched on the site and kept booking links, which are shared rather than searched, on the app; profiles and city pages are searched. On both hosts, links from other sites would split between two addresses. The site holds no sign-in cookies, and a profile reads nothing about its visitor, so one cached copy serves everybody.

## D-110 | 2026-09-15 | What a public profile shows, and what it keeps back
- **Decision:** `instructor_profile_page` returns a fixed list of fields, and only for an instructor the platform has approved whose Business is active; a test pins the list, so a new column is private until it is added on purpose. The area covered is drawn around the base point rounded to two decimal places, about a kilometre, and named by its district (LS6), never the postcode. An instructor whose badge is out of date keeps the profile, marked Not taking new bookings, with no times offered (INS-03). Free times are the next three bookable half hours within 14 days, for the shortest lesson on offer, held to the booking rules themselves; each opens the booking link with that time chosen. The profile is kept for hours and expired by tag when the instructor changes the profile, the area, the hours or the rules, when a Business changes its booking rules, and when a verification decision is made; the free times are kept for about a minute. Reviews are not shown, because reviews are Phase 2 (REV-01 to REV-05). Join waiting list arrives with the learner capture in M5-10, because a Business's own waiting list is Phase 2 (GAP-01).
- **Options:** Draw the area around the exact base; take the profile down while a badge is out of date; work the free times out on every visit; build the Phase 2 waiting list now.
- **Reason:** The base is often the instructor's home, and a circle several miles wide loses nothing by starting a kilometre away. A learner who finds an instructor through a shared link should learn why they cannot book rather than meet a missing page. Free times take hundreds of rule checks to find, and the booking page checks a time again anyway, so a minute's copy is safe. Showing reviews or a waiting list before they exist would promise a feature the platform does not have.

## D-111 | 2026-09-15 | Structured data from what the page shows, checked against schema.org's own definitions
- **Decision:** Each public page writes JSON-LD built in `packages/core/src/structured-data.ts` from what the page already shows. An instructor is a Person who provides a Service, with an Offer for each price, each offer pointing at the booking link. A school is both a LocalBusiness, as PUB-02 asks, and an EducationalOrganization, with its instructors as employees and an Offer for each of its prices. Every page carries a BreadcrumbList from home through the city page, when its city has one, to itself. There is no AggregateRating until reviews exist (Phase 2), and no street address, which the platform does not publish. Prices are written as schema.org expects ("42.00"), worked out in whole pence. The script escapes "<", so nothing typed into a profile can end it early. A unit test checks the output against a slice of schema.org's published vocabulary kept in the repository: every type must be a schema.org type, and every property must belong to the node's type or one above it.
- **Options:** Use `DrivingSchool` as the plan named it; check the output against Google's Rich Results Test by hand; describe a school as a LocalBusiness alone.
- **Reason:** schema.org has no `DrivingSchool` type, so markup using it would be ignored or flagged; EducationalOrganization is the nearest real type, and a node may have two. A test against the vocabulary itself fails the build when a property is misplaced, which a manual check cannot do on every change. Markup that says only what the page shows keeps search results honest and can never publish something private.

## D-112 | 2026-09-15 | Sharing the booking link: a QR code made on the device, and Instagram through the share sheet
- **Decision:** The instructor's Profile screen starts with Your booking link: the link with Copy link, WhatsApp (a wa.me link carrying a short message with the link), Instagram, a QR code of the link with a download as an SVG file for printing, and a link to the public profile. It says why there is no link while the badge waits for approval or has run out. Instagram takes no link from a web page, so where the device has a share sheet (the Web Share API, which every current phone has) the button opens it, and Instagram is in it; anywhere else it copies the link and says to paste it into a bio or story. The QR code is made in `packages/core/src/share.ts` with `uqr` (MIT, no dependencies, pinned), with medium error correction and the four-module quiet zone the standard asks for, black on white whatever the brand colours are. A unit test and a Playwright test decode it with `jsQR`, the second from what the page actually draws, to prove it scans to the booking link. The booking link page itself now shows the instructor's photo with the Verified tick, links to the full profile, is named for the instructor and kept out of search (the profile is the page to find, D-109), and opens on the first day that has a free time rather than on a day with none.
- **Options:** Link straight to Instagram, which has no way to share a web link from a browser; draw the QR code on the server as a PNG; use an online QR image service; write the QR encoder here; open the booking page on today, as before.
- **Reason:** A QR code made on the device needs no request, stores nothing and leaks no link to a third party, and an SVG prints sharp at any size, from a business card to a car window. Decoding it in the tests means a broken code fails the build instead of a learner's camera. The share sheet is the only way a phone lets a web page hand a link to Instagram. Opening the booking page on a day with nothing free made the first thing a learner saw a dead end.

## D-113 | 2026-09-15 | Being listed is the instructor's choice, and search works out the rest
- **Decision:** `is_listed` is now only the instructor's own choice, Show me in search on their Profile screen (PUB-04). Whether search may show a profile is worked out in one place, `private.instructor_in_search`: approved, a badge in date or none recorded, listed, and a Business in good standing. The public profile carries it as `inSearch`, and a profile out of search tells search engines not to index it; the city pages and sitemaps use the same rule (M5-07, M5-08). An expired badge no longer switches `is_listed` off. The daily job still notices each badge that runs out, once, now recorded as the last milestone of its set (0 days before) beside the reminders, and still writes the audit line and the event; it also has the kept copies of public profiles read fresh. Profiles the old job had taken out came back when this change was applied, since nobody could hide a profile by hand before it, and badges that had already run out were marked as noticed, so nobody is told twice. A profile hidden from search keeps its booking link; only an out of date badge pauses the link, as before (INS-03). The setup checklist's booking link item follows the badge, not the listing.
- **Options:** Keep switching `is_listed` off when a badge runs out, and switch it back on when a new badge is approved; add a second column for the reason a profile is hidden; take a hidden profile's page down altogether.
- **Reason:** One switch doing two jobs could not tell a profile somebody hid on purpose from one hidden by the calendar, so a renewal would either put back a profile its instructor wanted hidden or leave a renewed instructor invisible until they found the switch. A rule worked out when search asks cannot fall out of step with the badge date, and it is the same rule everywhere a profile is listed. PUB-04 asks for the booking link to keep working while hidden, and a learner holding the link should still reach the instructor.

## D-114 | 2026-09-15 | A place page lists the instructors based there, and stays out of search below three
- **Decision:** `/driving-lessons/<city>`, `/driving-lessons/<city>/<area>` and `/driving-lessons/<city>/automatic` list the instructors search may show (D-113) whose base postcode is in the place, by its district (D-108): a city page lists everyone based in any of its districts, an area page those based in that district, and the automatic page those in the city who teach automatic or both. `city_page` returns a fixed list of fields for each, as the profile does. Any place page listing fewer than 3 carries `noindex, follow`, city and automatic pages as well as areas, from one threshold in `packages/core/src/places.ts`; the page still shows, with its links. A city that is not a launch city, or an area that is not in the city, is not found; a district of a launch city with nobody in it has its page, saying nobody is listed yet. The city page links each area with somebody listed, with how many, and its automatic page when somebody teaches automatic; area and automatic pages link back to the city, and profiles and school pages link to their city page. Every place page has a breadcrumb, on the page and as a BreadcrumbList. The pages are kept for hours and read fresh whenever any instructor changes what their profile shows, or all profiles are (a verification decision, the badge job).
- **Options:** List an instructor on every place their travel radius reaches; apply the threshold to area pages alone, as PRD 8.3 words it; link every district from the city page, empty ones included; expire only the pages of the city an instructor was in before and after a change.
- **Reason:** A radius reaches several areas, so the same instructors would fill page after page, and near-identical pages are what search engines treat as thin; the base puts each instructor on one area page and makes the count mean something. A city or automatic page with one or two instructors is as thin as an area page with one or two. Links to empty areas would send learners, and search engines, to pages that say nobody is there. Working out which pages list an instructor before and after a change to their base, listing or transmission would need the old values in every Server Action that changes them; one tag for all place pages is always right, and the pages cost one query each to read again.

## D-115 | 2026-09-15 | Sitemaps by kind of page, and a share image drawn for each public page
- **Decision:** `/sitemap.xml` is an index of four sitemaps, one for each kind of public page: `/sitemaps/pages.xml` for the site's own pages (the marketing pages join it in M5-09), `instructors.xml` for every profile search may show (D-113), `schools.xml` for schools in good standing with at least one instructor in search, and `places.xml` for city, area and automatic pages that list at least 3 (D-114). Each lists whole addresses on the public site, with no dates, read from `sitemap_entries`, kept for hours and read fresh with the place pages. A sitemap past 50,000 addresses fails rather than dropping pages; the index would then list that kind in parts. robots.txt in production names the index. A school page with nobody in search is out of search, as it is out of the sitemap. Every public page has its canonical address on the public site, and Open Graph and card tags. Instructor, school and place pages each have a share image, 1200 by 630, drawn by the app at `<page>/share.png?v=<version>` from words in `packages/core/src/share-card.ts`: who or where, the facts a learner decides on first, the photo or initials with the Verified tick, and what to do there. The version changes whenever anything drawn changes, and an image is kept for a day. A booking link previews with its instructor's image. The image uses Inter from font files kept beside the app (Open Font License), and a photo, kept as WebP, is turned into a PNG with sharp first, since the renderer cannot read WebP; a photo that cannot be fetched in three seconds is drawn as initials.
- **Options:** Next's `sitemap.ts` file in each folder, which makes no index and scatters the sitemaps under each prefix; `opengraph-image.tsx` files, whose address stays the same for a whole build, are kept for a year by default, and could not be named from the booking link on the app's host; initials only, with no photo, and no sharp; `lastmod` dates from each row's `updated_at`.
- **Reason:** An index with one sitemap for each kind is what search consoles report on kind by kind, and one list of the site's own pages keeps the host rules and the sitemap in step. WhatsApp and Facebook keep a link's preview for weeks, so an image address that changes with its drawing is the only way a renamed instructor or a new price reaches the preview. A photo is what makes a shared instructor link look like a person, and sharp is the usual way to read WebP on a server; Next already depends on it for images. Dates would be wrong: free times and prices change without the profile's row changing, and search engines ignore dates they learn not to trust.

## D-116 | 2026-09-16 | The site's own pages sell only what is built, with prices from the configuration
- **Decision:** The public site has its own layout for the home page, `/learners`, `/instructors-software`, `/driving-schools-software` and `/pricing`, and the profiles and place pages share it: a header with the four pages, Sign in and Get started (on a phone the pages sit behind a Menu disclosure and the page's own button is the one to press), and a footer that links every launch city's page, read from the database. Every price, the founding offer's limits and Stripe's standard UK card fee come from `@repo/config/plans`, so the pages change with the configuration. Features are listed as the plan has them today, and PRD 9.18's features that arrive in Phase 2 (Gap Fill, waiting list automation, expenses and exports, calendar sync, custom booking colours, school reports and fleet) are listed apart under Coming later, never as included. Learner search is described as coming soon, with links to the city pages until it arrives. The pages show the app as it is: its screens drawn in its own components and tokens rather than screenshots or stock photographs, hidden from screen readers since the words beside them say the same. Headlines on a wide screen use one new type size, `text-hero` (56 px), which nothing inside the app uses. The site's own pages share one share image of the site. The card fee is 1.5% plus 20p, stated as Stripe's and as the Business's cost (D-078).
- **Options:** List PRD 9.18's plan contents exactly as written; show real screenshots or photographs; keep the design system's largest size, 36 px, for headlines; a sticky header with the main button on every width.
- **Reason:** Listing a feature that does not exist yet as part of a plan would mislead the instructors and schools choosing it, and anybody who paid for Pro in Phase 2 would find half of it missing; Coming later keeps the plan honest and the direction visible. Screens drawn from the app's own components stay sharp at every width, weigh almost nothing against the Lighthouse target (M5-24), and cannot fall out of date the way a screenshot does. A 36 px headline reads as a form heading on a wide marketing page; one extra size, kept to the site, fixes that without touching the app's scale. One button per screen is the app's rule (PRD 7.1), and on a phone the header's copy of the main button sat directly above the page's own.

## D-117 | 2026-09-16 | Coming soon keeps a learner's details only with consent, and cannot be turned on anybody else
- **Decision:** The learner marketplace opens one postcode area at a time, in `public.marketplace_regions`, where an area with no row is closed (PRD 4.1, 4.2; Super Admin opens areas in M5-19). Where an area is closed, a learner gives a postcode on the learners page, or on their home screen while they have no lessons, and joins the area's waiting list or posts a lesson request (MKT-10): the transmission, how far along they are, the days and times of day that suit, when to start, and optionally a mobile and the most they would pay for an hour. Each is kept only when the consent box is ticked, with the exact words shown next to it and the moment it was ticked; an address has one place on an area's list and one open request there, which posting again replaces. Answers are the same whether an address was already there, so nobody can use the form to learn who is on a list. Because anybody may call these functions straight through the API, the functions guard themselves: each counts its caller (the account when signed in, otherwise the address the edge reports) and everybody together, within an hour (30 area checks and 10 entries a caller, 3000 and 1000 for everybody), and an address is sent at most one confirmation email a day, whatever it asks for. The confirmation's one button leads to a page that shows what is kept for that address, by kind and area alone, and removes all of it with one press; the removal is a button on the page, not the link, because mail scanners open links. The postcode need not be in the shared cache, which a visitor may not write to: the app looks it up upstream to check it exists and find its city page, after the database has counted the look. A profile not taking new bookings points to this, since a Business's own waiting list is Phase 2 (GAP-01, D-110). The consent promises an email when the area opens, so opening an area in M5-19 must email its waiting list.
- **Options:** Rate limit in the Server Action with the service key's limiter; let visitors add to the postcode cache; send a confirmation for every entry; remove one entry per link; ask for a double opt-in before keeping anything.
- **Reason:** The service key stays with webhooks, jobs and seeds (CLAUDE.md), and a limit a caller can step around by calling the API directly is no limit. A visitor writing to the cache could plant wrong coordinates for a real postcode that everybody's coverage then uses. A form that sends an email to any address on request is a way to fill a stranger's inbox, so the email is capped per address rather than per caller. Withdrawing consent has to be as easy as giving it (UK GDPR), and a learner who never asked for any of it should undo all of it at once. A second confirmation step before keeping anything would lose most learners for a list that holds nothing sensitive, while the cap and the one-press removal already protect somebody whose address was used without asking.

## D-118 | 2026-09-16 | A school is set up before its portal opens, and its instructors join through a link sent from the owner's phone
- **Decision:** A new school's owner turns on two-step verification, then sets the school up on two screens before its portal opens: its name, a logo, its main postcode (looked up by the server, never coordinates from the browser) and roughly how many instructors teach there; then a link for each instructor. A Business now carries `onboarding_completed_at`, set when it is made for a Business of one and when the owner finishes for a school; every Business already there counts as set up. An invitation to teach is a member invitation in the same table as a learner's (D-064): `invite_member` stores only the token's hash, and invites instructors only, until a school can say what a manager may do (SCH-02). The owner sends each link from their own phone by text, WhatsApp or email with the words already written, and the screen lists who has been invited as Waiting, Joined or Expired. The link's page names the school. Signed out, it leads to an instructor sign-up with the name and email filled in, and the new account joins the school instead of becoming a Business of one; signed in, one tap joins. The instructor then verifies their mobile like any instructor and sets up in four steps, because the school sets the prices (SCH-04). An account that already teaches for another Business cannot join a school and is told why; somebody already at the school who opens the link is told it is for somebody else. A logo is kept in the avatars bucket in a folder of the school's own, which storage lets only those who may change the school's profile write. An invitation remembered through sign-up is now kept until it is used or the person has a role, so a learner who signs up with Google and chooses a role afterwards is still linked to their instructor.
- **Options:** Send each instructor a text from the platform's own number through Twilio, as PRD 10.5 describes ("Each gets an SMS"); let one account teach for several Businesses; keep the prices step for an instructor at a school; open the portal first and set the school up from a checklist.
- **Reason:** A text from the platform costs money per message, needs a registered sender and arrives from a number the instructor does not know, while a text from the owner's own phone comes from somebody they already know and costs nothing. It is how instructors already invite learners (M2-03), and texts from the platform stay for reminders (NTF-01). The owner still invites by phone number, and the instructor still gets a text. Every instructor screen assumes one diary, one Business and one set of prices, so a second Business on one account would show the wrong one until there is a way to switch between them, which no task in the plan builds; refusing clearly is better than showing a school instructor another Business's diary. A school's prices apply to all its instructors (SCH-04), so asking an instructor for their own would be a question with no effect. A portal opened before the school has a postcode would show an empty Overview and leave allocation (SCH-03) nothing to work from.
- **Owner's answer, 17 September 2026:** one account teaches for one Business, confirmed. Somebody who already teaches elsewhere and wants to teach for a school signs up with a different email; the invitation screen and the refusal both say so. Inviting managers waits: an owner invites instructors only for now.

## D-119 | 2026-09-16 | What the school overview counts, and who sees its revenue
- **Decision:** The school portal opens on Overview (SCH-01). Lessons today and this week count lessons on, under way, done or missed, by when they start in London, Monday to Sunday. Revenue this month is money taken in the calendar month for lessons and fees and for packages, less money given back: the Money screen's paid, credit sold and refunds in one figure, with its parts underneath (D-096). Unpaid is everything owed now, however old: lessons that have started and are not paid, and fees owed. Utilisation is the time an instructor's lessons take this week against the time they are open for lessons, which is their working hours and extra hours less time off. Lessons outside open hours count too, so a week can be over 100%, and a week with no open hours has no figure. New learners are those the school took on this calendar month. One database function works the figures out for a given moment, which the tests hold still across the October clock change, and the function the app calls checks who is asking and uses now. The owner, managers and platform staff see the overview; revenue only the owner, staff, and a manager given `view_revenue` (PRD 6.2), which the owner will be able to give with the permissions in M5-13. Instructors at the school do not see it. The Money screen still shows managers the school's money, which M5-16 brings into line with this.
- **Options:** Count only booked time inside open hours, capped at 100%; show unpaid for this month only, as the Money screen does for a period; show paid, packages and refunds as three figures; work the figures out in the app from several queries.
- **Reason:** A lesson booked outside somebody's hours still uses their day, and an owner should see an instructor working beyond their hours rather than a flat 100%. The overview answers what the school is owed, where the Money screen already shows what went unpaid in any period. One figure for the month with its parts beneath reads at a glance, and the Money screen has the detail. One function gives figures that agree with each other at one instant, and lets the tests fix the clock, the 25 hour day included.

## D-120 | 2026-09-16 | A member is switched off, not removed, and two permissions say what members may do
- **Decision:** The school's Instructors screen lists its instructors, invitations still waiting, managers and anybody switched off, and each person opens a sheet with what can be changed about them (SCH-02). A member is switched off rather than removed: the membership stays, marked off, so lessons, records and history keep their name, and the school can switch them back on. Every access check already asks for an active membership, so a member switched off loses the school at once. Their public address is withdrawn, which takes their profile and booking link off the public site and out of city pages, the school's page and the sitemaps, and the booking rules refuse any new lesson with them, whoever books it (INSTRUCTOR_INACTIVE). Lessons already booked stay in the school's diary for the school to give to another instructor or call off. Switched back on, they get a public address made from their name again, and somebody who has meanwhile started teaching for another Business stays off (D-118). The owner and managers switch instructors off and on; only the owner switches managers; nobody switches themselves or the owner. What a member may do beyond their role is two permissions on the membership: `set_own_prices` for an instructor, the pricing control that M5-15's instructor prices will read, and `view_revenue` for a manager (PRD 6.2), which only the owner may change. Nobody changes their own. An invitation can be cancelled until somebody uses it, and its link stops working at once. Every change is in the audit log.
- **Options:** Delete the membership; leave the public profile up until somebody hides it; refuse to switch off an instructor with lessons still to come; name the instructor's permission `set_prices`, as ARCHITECTURE's example does; show every control on every row of the list.
- **Reason:** Deleting a membership would leave lessons and records naming somebody who is not there, and a school taking a person back should not have to invite them again. Learners must not book, or be booked with, somebody who has left, and withdrawing the address covers every public page at once, since each already leaves out a profile without one. A school usually has lessons booked ahead when somebody leaves, and making it move them all before cutting access is the wrong order when somebody leaves on bad terms. `set_prices` already lets whoever holds it change the whole Business's catalogue, so an instructor given it could change every instructor's prices; a key of its own lets M5-15 give an instructor their own prices and nothing more. A school of twenty instructors would have forty controls on one screen, where one tap in the wrong place switches somebody off.

## D-121 | 2026-09-16 | How instructors are suggested for a learner
- **Decision:** Giving a learner to an instructor at a school opens a sheet that suggests up to three instructors, best first, each with its reasons, above everybody else still teaching there, who can all be chosen by hand (SCH-03, LRN-06). The database gathers the facts for each instructor at the school who has not been switched off: whether their badge is checked and in date, the gearbox they teach, the distance from the learner's postcode (or home pickup when the profile has none) to their base, how far they travel, and over the next 14 days the time open for lessons (working hours and extra hours, less time off) and the part of it not taken by lessons, requests still waiting and their buffers. `packages/core/src/allocation.ts` puts them in order: nobody who does not teach the learner's gearbox is suggested; then instructors whose badge is checked and in date; then those whose area covers the learner, before those with no base or no learner postcode, before those who do not reach; then those with time for at least a one hour lesson; then the most free time; then the nearest; then by name. The reasons say the same in words: a badge not checked or out of date, Covers M13, 2.1 miles away, or how far beyond their radius, 12 free hours in the next 2 weeks or fully booked or no working hours, and the gearbox they teach. The list cannot be tapped until it has settled, so a name never moves under a finger. Nobody can be given to an instructor who has been switched off (D-120).
- **Options:** A weighted score mixing distance, free time and gearbox; suggest instructors who do not teach the gearbox, lower down; count free time over a week or a month; place a learner by their pickup point rather than their postcode; count how many learners each instructor already has.
- **Reason:** An order made of plain steps can be explained in the reasons beside it and tested one step at a time, where a score needs its weights defended and cannot be read off the screen. A learner who wants an automatic cannot learn in a manual car, so suggesting one would only be a way to get it wrong. Two weeks is how far ahead most lessons are booked; a month would count hours nobody books yet. The postcode is what every learner gives, and most pickups are home. A count of learners says little without knowing how often each has lessons, which free time already reflects.

## D-122 | 2026-09-17 | One price per lesson, an instructor's own over the school's; packages and cancellation stay the school's
- **Decision:** A school sets its prices for each lesson type and length, its packages, and its booking and cancellation rules on its Settings screen, and they apply to everybody who teaches there (SCH-04). An instructor the school allows (set_own_prices, D-120) sets their own price on any lesson from their own Settings screen, and it wins over the school's wherever a price is shown or charged. One rule in the database, `private.effective_lesson_prices`, now feeds the booking link, the profile, the school's page and the city pages; before, the first two listed both prices for the same lesson and the pages took the lower of them, while booking already charged the instructor's. A blank price takes a lesson off for the school, or goes back to the school's price for an instructor. Taking the permission away removes that instructor's own prices at once, which the audit log records. Prices are saved together or not at all, from £5 to £1,000 a lesson, in whole quarter hours. Packages and the cancellation policy stay the school's alone, and each instructor keeps their own gap between lessons and whether bookings are confirmed at once, as before (M1-18). An instructor on their own now sets their Business's prices and packages on the same screens; before, only onboarding set them.
- **Options:** Let instructors set their own packages and cancellation policy too; keep an instructor's prices when the permission is taken away and ignore them until it is given back; build separate price screens for instructors on their own later.
- **Reason:** Credit is held with the Business (PAY-12) and used with any of its instructors, so a package priced by instructor would make the same hour of credit worth different amounts depending on who teaches it. A late cancellation fee is the Business's money, and a learner should meet one policy per Business. Prices kept but ignored would come back unseen, perhaps long out of date, if the permission were given again; removing them makes the switch mean exactly what it says. One rule read everywhere means the price on a profile is the price charged. The screens had to exist for schools, and an instructor on their own had no way to change a price after onboarding, which the same screens fix.
- **Owner's answer, 17 September 2026:** agreed. An instructor overrides the school's prices only; packages and the cancellation policy stay the school's.

## D-123 | 2026-09-17 | Payouts and billing are the owner's, down to the columns
- **Decision:** The columns of a Business that describe its payouts and its plan (`stripe_payouts_enabled`, `stripe_details_submitted`, `stripe_connected_at`, `plan`, `plan_expires_at` and `founding_offer`) are no longer readable by anybody signed in. Everybody who may see a Business reads the rest, including its payments account id and whether it takes cards, which a learner paying by card needs; a column added later is private until it is granted. The owner, and a platform super admin past their second step, read the account through `payments_account` and the plan through `business_billing`. The Money screen shows the card payments panel only to the owner, and asks the payments provider about the account only on the owner's behalf. `money_summary` gives a manager the school's takings (paid, credit sold and refunds) only when the owner allows it (`view_revenue`, D-120), and always what is unpaid; an instructor still sees what their own lessons took. acceptance-11 is green at both widths, and pgTAP proves the refusals at the columns and the functions.
- **Options:** Hide the panel from managers in the page and change nothing underneath; read the Business through a view without the payout columns; leave the plan readable until there are billing screens.
- **Reason:** A manager with a session can call the API directly, so hiding a panel leaves payouts one request away; column privileges refuse the read wherever it comes from, and the two functions give the owner exactly what the screen needs. A view would mean changing every existing query to read it, and the table itself would still be readable. The plan is what a school pays the platform, which PRD 6.2 keeps from managers as billing, and closing it now is cheaper than finding every reader later.

## D-124 | 2026-09-17 | What the admin dashboard counts, over the last 30 days
- **Decision:** The dashboard covers the 30 days up to the moment it is opened, and says which days. Sign-ups are accounts made in those days and confirmed by email or phone, by the role chosen at sign-up, with those yet to choose counted apart; a learner an instructor added is not a sign-up until they claim the account (D-068). Active Businesses are those in good standing, as independent instructors and schools; Teaching is those with a lesson on, under way, done or missed in the 30 days; Suspended is counted apart. Lessons booked are those made in the 30 days that were not declined or left to lapse, even if called off since; lessons completed are those that started in the 30 days and were marked done. GMV is every payment taken in the 30 days, by card, cash or bank, before refunds, with how much was by card and what was refunded underneath; credit is never counted, since the package that bought it already was. Fees earned are the platform's fees on those payments, which stay at nothing until marketplace fees start (Phase 3). Badges waiting and no-show disputes open are counted now, with the day the oldest has waited since; a badge links to the queue, and a dispute has no link because its Business decides it (D-091) and the staff disputes screen is ADM-08, Phase 2. One database function works it out for a given moment, so the test holds the clock still, and the function the page calls refuses anybody but platform staff past their second step. The admin shell was already desktop only and behind two-step verification (M0, AUTH-08). The figure tile moved out of the school overview so both dashboards share it, and counts are grouped by thousands (`formatCount`), since a platform's run into them where a school's rarely do.
- **Options:** A calendar month like the school overview; a choice of 7, 30 or 90 days; GMV after refunds; counting a sign-up when the account is made rather than confirmed.
- **Reason:** A rolling 30 days reads the same on the 1st as on the 30th, where a calendar month starts every month at nothing; a choice of periods can come when somebody asks for it. GMV is gross by its usual meaning, and refunds sit beside it rather than inside it. An unconfirmed account is often a typo or a bot, and counting it would flatter the number the dashboard exists to show honestly.

## D-125 | 2026-09-17 | What suspending a Business does, and who may
- **Decision:** Platform staff past their second step find Businesses by name, postcode, or the name, email or mobile of anybody who works there, and open one to see its standing, figures and people; only a super admin suspends or reactivates one, and a suspension needs a reason, kept with the Business (never shown to it) and in the audit log with who and when. A suspended Business takes no bookings and no card payments from anybody: the refusal (`BUSINESS_SUSPENDED`) sits in `slot_problem`, which every new booking, move, recurring lesson and list of free times already passes through, and in the hold a lesson's card payment starts with, and the purchase screen sells no packages. Its booking link says it is not taking bookings, and its public pages, place pages and sitemap entries go at once. The people who work there lose its portal, as they already lost its permissions (M0), and signing in they are told the Business is suspended and how to reach support, rather than being offered a role to choose; nor can they start another Business meanwhile. Lessons already booked stay booked, and nothing is refunded or cancelled automatically. Reactivating puts everything back. The destructive button now darkens on hover rather than fading, since faded to 90% its white on red fell under 4.5:1, which the suspend question's accessibility scan caught.
- **Options:** Let support staff suspend as well; cancel and refund a suspended Business's lessons to come; keep the portal open read-only for its members; block only learners' bookings.
- **Reason:** PRD 6.2 gives support staff reading, verification, disputes and reviews, and suspension shuts down somebody's livelihood, so it stays with the founders for now. Cancelling and refunding automatically moves money on a decision that may be reversed the same day, and a Business's lessons are often paid in cash that the platform cannot give back; staff and the Business can sort out lessons case by case. The M0 rules already refuse a suspended Business's members every permission, so a read-only portal would be a screen of buttons that fail. A Business that can still book for its own learners is not suspended in any sense a learner would recognise.

## D-126 | 2026-09-17 | Suspending an account and resetting two-step verification
- **Decision:** Platform staff find instructors by the name on their profile or account, email, mobile or badge number, and learners by name, email or mobile, and open anybody, including from a Business's people, to see their email, mobile, whether two-step verification is on, when they joined and last signed in, where they work and learn, and any suspension. Only a super admin suspends or reactivates an account, with a reason kept from the person and written to the audit log, or resets somebody's two-step verification; never their own, and nobody on the platform staff is suspended from here.
