- **Decision:** The sign-up form has a tick, never ticked for anybody: "I agree to the Terms for learners (or Terms for instructors) and have read the Privacy notice", each a link opening beside the form. Nothing is made without it. The version ticked is sent with the account and written down as it is made. Anybody else (someone who signed up with Google or Apple, an account made for them by an instructor, an account from before this) is sent to `/agree` before their portal or onboarding opens, and agrees there through `accept_terms`, which is audited. Staff, and staff viewing as somebody, are not asked.
- **Versions:** `packages/core/src/legal.ts` holds each document's date, which the pages print, and the version agreed to is the newest of them. Changing any of the three documents' dates asks everybody to agree again on their next visit. `public.users` keeps `terms_version` and `terms_accepted_at`, together or not at all; nobody can write them except through the sign-up or `accept_terms`.
- **Options:** A tick on the sign-up form only (leaves Google sign-ups and existing accounts out); a screen for everybody, sign-ups included (asks twice); a tick at sign-up and a screen for everybody else (chosen).
- **Test accounts:** the seed and the database tests' helpers make accounts that agreed, as the people they stand for would have, and a test holds the helpers to the current version.

## D-266 | 2026-10-14 | Prices skipped in onboarding are set from Settings, the same way | Product owner, Claude
- **Found:** the product owner skipped prices in onboarding, and Settings then showed a "Save prices" button with nothing above it. The Prices card lists a price for each lesson and length, and somebody who skipped has no lesson yet: onboarding's prices screen is what makes the first one, from an hourly price.
- **Decision:** With no lesson yet, the card says so and asks for the hourly price, and the package if they sell one, with onboarding's own form and the same `set_onboarding_prices`. Saved, it makes the standard lesson at 1, 1.5 and 2 hours as onboarding does, and the card shows the full table to change one by one. For an independent instructor, as the RPC is; a school's prices are set by the school.
- **Options:** Default prices put in for them (prices nobody chose, shown to learners); an empty table with a row per length (a second way to start, unlike onboarding's); onboarding's form in the card (chosen).

## D-267 | 2026-10-14 | Pass photos are shown whole, at their own shape | Product owner, Claude
- **Found:** the product owner: the gallery cropped the uploaded photo. The picture was always kept whole (D-218), but every wall drew it in a box four wide and three high and cut off whatever did not fit, so a photo taken upright on a phone, which is most of them, lost its top and bottom: a head, or the certificate.
- **Decision:** Each photo is drawn whole at its own shape: on the public profile, on the instructor's and the school's own wall, and on the staff wall, where the certificate is what is being checked. The walls are laid out in columns rather than rows, so an upright photo beside a sideways one leaves no gap; the newest is still first, at the top of the first column. No photo is taller than most of the screen, and one far taller than it is wide is shown whole inside that.
- **Size:** the browser already knows the size of the picture it prepared, and now sends it. `gallery_photos` keeps `image_width` and `image_height`, both or neither, and the walls hand them back, so a page keeps the right room for a photo before it loads instead of moving everything under it as it arrives, which would count against the public pages. Photos added before this have no size: they keep a four by three room until they load, then take their own shape.
- **Options:** keep the box and fit the whole photo inside it (whole, but small and with bars); draw each at its own shape without knowing its size (the page jumps as each one loads); its own shape, with the size written down (chosen).

## D-268 | 2026-10-14 | Three pass photos on a profile, the checked ones first, and the whole gallery on its own page | Product owner, Claude
- **Asked for:** the product owner: the gallery on a public profile should show three photos, the verified ones first where there are any, with a "View full gallery" button opening a page or a popup that scrolls through the rest. A profile showed up to 24, newest first.
- **Decision:** A profile, an instructor's or a school's, shows three: the ones staff have ticked first, newest first among them, and the newest of the rest after. Three across from the tablet width up, one to a row on a phone. Where the wall has more than three, "View full gallery" opens it on a page of its own under the profile (`/instructors/<city>/<slug>/gallery`, and the same under a school), with every photo still up, newest first, in columns, a link back named after the profile, and how many there are. A wall of more than 120 shows the newest 120 and says so; paging can follow when a wall gets near it.
- **Page, not popup:** a page works before the JavaScript arrives, keeps the profile light (the photos load only when somebody asks for them), has an address that can be shared, and goes back with the back button. It is in search when the profile is, with the profile's share image.
- **Data:** `instructor_gallery` and `school_gallery` take `p_whole` (default false), so a call with the slug alone, as the app makes it until it is deployed, is still answered, with the three. They also return `total`, the photos still up, so the profile shows the button only when there is a rest. The order is decided in the database, since only it sees the whole wall.
- **Deploy:** this time the database goes first: every function the old app calls still answers after the migration, but the new app asks for `p_whole` and `total`, which only the new functions have.

## D-269 | 2026-10-14 | An independent instructor's profile goes by their Business name, at an address made from it | Product owner, Claude
- **Asked for:** the product owner: an instructor's public profile should show their Business name as the main name, with their own name still visible above it in very small grey type, and use the Business name for the link.
- **Decision:** For somebody running their own Business, the profile's heading is the Business name, with their own name small and grey above it (left out where the two are the same). The page title, the breadcrumb, the share image and the whole gallery page go by the same name; the photo's initials, and the person in the structured data, stay theirs. The profile's address, and so the booking link, is made from the Business name (`/instructors/leeds/sarah-khan-driving`, `/book/sarah-khan-driving`), and follows it when the Business is renamed.
- **Old links keep working:** this replaces "renaming leaves the slug alone" in D-196, whose reason still holds: every address a profile has had is kept (`private.former_profile_slugs`) and leads to the one it has now, for the profile, its gallery, its share image and the booking link, with the time a booking link was opened for. An old address is never handed to anybody else, an instructor can no longer set an address by hand (one set by hand could take somebody else's old one), and the old addresses go with an account when it is deleted. The migration moved every existing independent instructor whose address was not their Business name. The redirect is drawn by the page, so a search engine reads it as the page saying it has moved, the same way an old city in a profile address already moves.
- **Schools:** an instructor at a school keeps their own name and an address made from it: they all work for the school, and the school has a page and an address of its own. The listings on the city pages still show the person, with their photo, since that is who teaches the lesson.
- **Options:** the Business name on the page only, with the address left alone (what was asked for, half done); a new address with the old one simply gone (breaks every card and message an instructor has given out); a new address, with every old one leading to it (chosen).

## D-270 | 2026-10-14 | The city and area lists, and the booking link, go by the Business name too | Product owner, Claude
- **Asked for:** the product owner, after D-269 went live: the London and Bexley pages still showed instructors by their own names. D-269 had left the lists showing the person.
- **Decision:** Every public place that names an instructor running their own Business now does it the same way as their profile: the Business name first, their own name small and grey above it. That is the city, area and automatic pages (`city_page` returns each instructor's Business name and kind, and the list is in the order of the names it shows) and the booking link's heading and title, where the Business is no longer repeated in the line under the heading. One rule decides it, `profileNames` in `packages/core`. An instructor at a school is still listed by their own name, and the confirmation after booking still says the lesson is with the person.
- **Deploy:** either order is safe: the app reads a list without the Business as before, by the person's name, and the database's extra field means nothing to the app before it.

## D-271 | 2026-10-18 | The jobs fit the job runner's free month | Product owner, Claude
- **Found:** emails stopped on 15 October. Resend had dropped doovor.com to Failed because its DKIM record had gone from the DNS at Hostinger, and refused every email from it; the product owner put the record back. Looking at Inngest on the way showed the account at 45,892 of the Hobby plan's 50,000 executions with 14 days of the month left: the outbox sweep and the notification sender each ran every minute, 1,440 runs a day apiece, and the other sweeps every five or ten minutes, about 4,200 runs a day in all. At that rate every job, emails, reminders, holds and charges included, stops within a day until the month turns.
- **Asked for:** the product owner stays on the free plan until the business earns, lets this month run out as it will, and wants the jobs to fit a month of it.
- **Decision:** notifications are sent by the job that writes them, in the same run (`thenSend`), so nothing waits for a sweep to send it. The notification sender's own sweep is gone; the outbox sweep, every three minutes, sends waiting events and tries again notifications that failed. The other sweeps run as often as what they do needs: reminders every five minutes (the nudge five minutes before a lesson needs that), holds every ten (a hold lasts fifteen), lapsed requests and their cards every fifteen (a request lasts hours), and the day-before charges every thirty. Together about 1,160 runs a day, 36,000 in a long month, leaving 14,000 for jobs that follow something somebody did. Every schedule is in `apps/web/src/jobs/schedules.ts`, and a test fails if they add up to more than 40,000 or if a job is given a schedule anywhere else.
- **Cost:** an event raised in the database reaches its job up to three minutes later, where it was up to one. An email about a booking arrives within about three minutes.
- **Options:** a paid plan (declined for now); a database trigger calling the app on every outbox row, so events go at once and the sweep could be rarer (needs an address and a secret kept in the database: worth doing when the plan is paid or usage grows); merging every sweep into one heartbeat (cheaper again, but one slow part holds up the rest).

## D-272 | 2026-10-18 | Back from Google without signing in, the page loads itself again | Product owner, Claude
- **Found:** the product owner: after "Continue with Google", coming back without finishing left the button turning as if still waiting, and signing in or up with the form then showed an error until the page was refreshed. The browser had brought the page back from its back-forward cache exactly as it was left, which phones above all do, with the button mid-press and a sign-in half started under the forms. Refreshing was what fixed it.
- **Decision:** a page that comes back that way after leaving for Google loads itself again at once (`pageshow` with `persisted`), so it is as fresh as after a refresh. If Google cannot be reached at all, the button stops turning and says so. The button is on the design page whatever this environment has switched on, which is where its test presses it: Google is off in the tests, so the trip there is answered with nothing and the browser's event is raised by hand. The test fails with the reload taken out.
- **Options:** resetting only the button (leaves the forms as they were left); telling browsers never to keep the sign-in pages (Safari keeps them regardless); a reload when the page comes back after leaving for Google (chosen).

## D-273 | 2026-10-18 | One icon for each thing, wherever it is shown | Product owner, Claude
- **Asked for:** the product owner: keep the icons consistent across the app. Payment setup had one icon on More and another on Money, and Tell us something one in the header and another on More; they prefer the More tab's.
- **Decision:** `apps/web/src/lib/icons.ts` holds the one icon for each thing reached from more than one place, and the menus, the navigation, page buttons and the header take them from there; the design page shows the set. As More shows them: Payment setup is a card, Tell us something a speech bubble with a plus (in the header too, and on the staff feedback inbox), Account and security a shield (the sidebar showed a person). Badges to check are the badge with a tick in all of the admin, where the shield had also meant verification. Learners are two people and instructors an ID card in every portal: two people had meant learners for an instructor and instructors for a school. A pickup point is a pin wherever it is added. Bookkeeping and referring look the same on their pages as on More. WhatsApp is the round bubble everywhere, a text message the square one, and in-app messages two bubbles, where WhatsApp and messages had shared one.
- **Rule:** before an icon is used for a thing in a second place, the thing goes in `icons.ts`.

## D-274 | 2026-10-18 | The sweeps that only change the database run inside it | Product owner, Claude
- **Asked for:** the product owner, after D-271: move the checks that only touch the database off the job runner, onto Supabase.
- **Decision:** lapsing requests nobody answered (`system_expire_requests`, R-12) and giving back slots whose payment hold ran out (`system_expire_payment_holds`, R-10) run on the database's own scheduler, pg_cron, every five minutes, at no cost to the job runner's month. Giving back a slot was only half of the hold sweep: it also called off the payment waiting behind the hold, at Stripe, which only the app can do. The database already raised `booking.hold_expired` for each booking it gave back, and nothing listened; now a job does, once per booking, reading what is still open from `system_hold_cancellations`, and is tried again if Stripe cannot be reached (the old sweep never tried again). The job runner's schedules come to about 28,500 runs in a long month.
- **Kept on the job runner:** everything that talks to Stripe, Resend or a phone, which is everything else that is scheduled: reminders, cards for requests that ran out, the day-before charges, and the morning jobs.
- **Options:** both sweeps whole on pg_cron, calling the app over HTTP for the Stripe part (needs an address and a secret kept in the database); leaving them on the job runner (D-271); the database part on pg_cron and the Stripe part following the event (chosen).

## D-275 | 2026-10-18 | The email logo looks meant in dark mode | Product owner, Claude
- **Found:** the product owner: in a phone's dark mode the logo at the top of an email showed as a light box with the black logo on it, while the rest of the email darkened well. The picture carried the email's grey painted in (D-249), so that a mail app darkening the background could not leave a black logo on black; the mail apps darken the rest and leave pictures alone, so the grey stayed.
- **Decision:** two pictures, the same size, of which a mail app shows one. By default a black logo on a white tile with rounded corners and clear corners, which in a mail app that darkens everything itself (Gmail, which cannot be told) reads as a tile that was meant. For a mail app that says it is dark (Apple Mail, Outlook), a white logo on a black tile instead, swapped in by `prefers-color-scheme` (and Outlook's `data-ogsc`); on a tile rather than on nothing, because such an app can still show the email's own light grey, and white on that would be lost. Nothing else in the email is told about dark mode, since the mail apps already darken it well, and saying the email supports dark mode would stop them. The sign-off logo in the footer is the same pair, smaller.
- **Auth's emails:** the four templates Supabase sends are rendered from the same layout, so they change too, and have to be put into Supabase again after the merge (RUNBOOK, Emails).

## D-276 | 2026-10-19 | A skill's rating opens under it on the record | Product owner, Claude
- **Found:** the product owner: on a lesson's record, tapping a skill seemed only to select it, and its 1 to 5 rating showed only after pressing Save, with the error that it had no rating. The ratings were listed above all 23 skills, so on a phone the rating of a skill tapped lower down opened above the screen, out of sight; the error took the page to the top, which is where they appeared. Skills tapped during the lesson also opened the record on the whole list rather than on them.
- **Decision:** on the record, a tapped skill's rating opens right under it, across the row, where the tap was. The skills tapped during the lesson are where the record starts: it opens on just those, each with its rating, and "Add or change skills" brings the whole list back. During the lesson the list stays as it was, skills only, to tap quickly.
- **Options:** taking the page up to the ratings after each tap (moves away from the list being chosen from); a sheet for each rating (one more step for every skill); the rating under the skill (chosen).

## D-277 | 2026-10-20 | A support number, shown only where a person may be needed | Product owner, Claude
- **Asked for:** the product owner gave the number that takes calls and WhatsApp messages: the support email can go anywhere it is needed, the number only where it is really needed.
- **Decision:** the number lives in `brand.ts` beside the support email, with a link that dials it and one that opens a WhatsApp chat, and the copy guard fails the build if it is written anywhere else, however it is spaced. It is shown in two places: the accessibility statement, where somebody who finds the product hard to use may need another way than writing in, and the page a suspended Business sees, which cannot take bookings or payments and may need a person sooner than an email brings one. Everywhere else, the footer, the legal pages and every email, keeps the email alone.
- **Options:** the number in the site footer as well (every visitor sees it, which is more calls than "really needed"); on the schools page for owners who would rather talk before signing up (left for the product owner to ask for).

## D-278 | 2026-10-20 | Schools work the way UK schools do | Product owner, Claude
- **Research:** "How UK Driving Schools Work" (20 Oct 2026, https://claude.ai/artifact/FoFZFsV7GgCzb5CDuoBf2Q). Most school instructors are self-employed and sell their own lessons, the school earning a weekly fee; HMRC's guidance for driving schools (VTAXPER66100, VTAXPER66400) makes the school the seller of every lesson when it allocates pupils, sets prices and takes the money, which is how the school side was built in M5 (D-011, SCH-03, SCH-04, SCH-05's default).
- **Owner's answers, 20 October 2026:** every recommendation accepted.
  1. A school's lessons are sold by the school, through its members (as built), or by linked instructors who keep their own Business, Stripe account, learners and prices; a school can have both.
  2. A school never holds learners' money to pay its instructors later.
  3. Payment routing (SCH-05) and the school's fees (SCH-09, which was Phase 5) come first.
  4. For linked instructors the school may recommend or cap prices, never fix them or set a minimum.
  5. When an instructor leaves, learners the school introduced may stay with them or move to another of the school's instructors, and an instructor's own learners go with them; the learner agrees either way.
  6. Prepaid hours pause and are refunded pro rata rather than running out.
  7. The School plan costs £6 an instructor a month, the lowest in the market (it was £9).
  8. An accountant and a solicitor answer the brief's questions before Doovor collects any money for a school.
- **Also from the owner:** Doovor does not check the badges of a school's instructors; the school vouches for them, and expiry reminders stay. Asked what happens to an instructor who already has an account: linked, they keep everything and nothing moves; as a member, their own Business pauses and their learners choose whether to move. Leaving works the same way round. This replaces the 17 September answer in D-118, under which they signed up again with another email.
- **Plan:** M9 in `docs/PLAN.md`, built on branch `m9-schools`.

## D-279 | 2026-10-20 | A school pays a fee of its own and one for each instructor it has | Product owner, Claude
- **Owner's answers, 20 October 2026:** the founding offer for schools stays: the first 50 schools pay nothing for a year. After that a school pays only for the instructors it really has, plus a monthly fee for the school itself. A linked instructor (M9), who keeps their own Business, is one of the school's instructors, and has Pro included while linked.
- **Decision:** £15 a month for the school plus £6 for each instructor, or £150 a year plus £60 for each instructor: by the year, two months free, as Pro's £120 against £144. No minimum, where the School plan used to charge for at least 2 instructors (`schoolFee`, `minimumInstructors: 0` in `packages/config/src/plans.ts`). Measured against the market in the schools research: 2 instructors £27 (Total Drive £24, DriveSchoolPro £32), 5 instructors £45 (Total Drive £60, Passly £51), 20 instructors £135 (Total Drive £200). The founding year covers both fees. The pricing pages say "£15 per month plus £6 per instructor, or £150 a year plus £60 per instructor".
- **Later:** counting the instructors when a bill is made, those teaching then and not an invitation still waiting or anybody switched off, arrives with the School plan's billing, which is not built yet (D-231). A linked instructor's Pro while linked arrives with M9-05.

## D-280 | 2026-10-20 | A school's instructors are vouched for by the school, not checked by Doovor | Product owner, Claude
- **Asked for:** the product owner (D-278): Doovor does not check the badges of a school's instructors; the school vouches for them.
- **Decision:** a profile made at a school is vouched for as it is made, by a trigger on `instructor_profiles`: approved, with `vouched_at` saying the school cleared it rather than Doovor. Being approved, it is live everywhere that already waits for approval, unchanged: search, the profile page, the booking link, open times, the school's page, the gallery and allocation. A school's instructor sends their badge's number and expiry with no photo, at onboarding or whenever they like from their profile, for the reminders before it runs out (INS-03) and what a trainee may do (INS-04); nobody at Doovor reviews it and nothing waits in the queue. School instructors who were waiting, or had sent nothing, were vouched for by the migration, their photos left for the nightly housekeeping (D-158); a badge Doovor had already decided on keeps its decision. One badge is still one account's (D-230). Doovor's tick and the words "checked by Doovor" are for a badge Doovor checked at a Business of one: a school's instructor's profile names the school instead, and the booking link says which with `checkedByDoovor`.
- **The seed** keeps Aisha Rahman at Quayside waiting from before, so the pages that hide somebody not yet cleared stay tested.
- **Options:** a new verification status, vouched, written into the eleven places that read approval; the school confirming each instructor before they go live, a step the owner did not ask for; Doovor still checking, as an option.

## D-281 | 2026-10-20 | A school says who sells its lessons | Product owner, Claude
- **Asked for:** D-278: a school's lessons are sold by the school through the instructors who teach under it, or by linked instructors each through their own Business, or some of each.
- **Decision:** the school says which, on `businesses.lesson_seller`: school, instructors or both. Three radio cards, "We sell the lessons", "Our instructors sell their own" and "Some of each", each saying in a line what it means for who learners pay, who sets the prices and who pays whom, and beneath them that it decides who learners pay and whose takings count for VAT, with a link to how HMRC sees driving schools (VTAXPER66100). A new school is asked when it is set up, with nothing chosen for it, so the answer is the owner's own; afterwards only the owner changes it, from Settings, through `set_lesson_seller`, as what the school pays is the owner's alone (PRD 6.2). A school cannot be only its instructors' while somebody still teaches under it: it is "Some of each" while they move to Businesses of their own. Every school there was before this sells its own lessons, which is all a school could do.
- **Options:** deciding it per instructor alone, by the kind of invitation sent (the school never says what it is, and HMRC asks what the school does); a yes or no for franchise schools (no room for a school moving from one to the other).

## D-282 | 2026-10-20 | A linked instructor keeps their own Business, linked to one school at a time | Product owner, Claude
- **Asked for:** D-278 and the owner's answers on joining and leaving: an instructor who sells their own lessons keeps everything when they join a school, and leaves with it.
- **Decision:** `school_links` joins a school to an instructor's own Business, which stays theirs: Stripe account, learners, prices, packages, receipts and history. A Business is linked to one school at a time. The school's owner and managers read the link, and so does the Business's owner; only database functions write it. The school invites with a third kind of invitation, sent the way its others are; the instructor accepts signed in, and what is linked is the Business they run on their own (somebody without one sets it up first; somebody who works for the school already is not linked to it as well). While linked, the school vouches for their badge as for its own (D-280) and their Business has Pro, paid for by the school's seat (D-279), with what it had before kept on the link. Either side ends it: the instructor leaves, or the school lets them go. Then the platform checks the badge again and the Business goes back to the plan it had, unless it now pays for Pro itself. A school with linked instructors cannot say it sells every lesson itself.
- **Later:** if a linked instructor who also paid for Pro themselves stops paying while linked, the plan follows their subscription rather than the link; and an instructor already paying for Pro is not yet told they need not while linked. Both arrive with the School plan's billing.
- **Options:** a member of the school's Business with their own Stripe account (one Business, two sellers, and credit tied to the school); several schools at once (a franchise is one brand).

## D-283 | 2026-10-20 | How an instructor's business is linked, seen and unlinked | Claude
- **Decision:** the school invites from its Instructors screen or while it is set up, as before, and the form offers only what the school's answer allows (D-281): to teach under it, to link a business of their own, or, for a school with both, a choice between them. The message for a link says what the instructor keeps. The link's page tells somebody signed in with a business of their own what linking means (their prices, payments and learners stay theirs; the school sends pupils and sees when they are free, never their money; the school vouches for their badge and their plan has Pro while linked; they can leave whenever they like) and links it in one tap. Somebody signed out creates an account, whose business is linked as soon as it exists, since the account was made from the link to link one; somebody with an account signs in and comes back to the page to say yes themselves. An account with no business of its own is told so. Linked, the instructor has Your school under More, with the school, since when, what it means and a way to leave, said first in words. The school's team is in sections by who sells its lessons: "Teaching under the school" and "Linked instructors", the second by the business each runs and since when, opening their contact and badge and a way to stop linking them, said first in words. Invitations waiting say which kind they are.
- **Options:** linking an existing account at sign-in without asking (the account was not made for it); a separate screen for linked instructors (two places to look for the same people).

## D-284 | 2026-10-20 | What a school sees of a linked instructor's diary | Claude
- **Decision:** the school's diary shows its linked instructors after its own, under "Linked instructors", with when each is taken that day and nothing more: a lesson of their own says "Booked", time off says "Away", a week away is "All day", and each says how long they are busy. Who the lesson is with, what kind it is, its price and the reason for time off never leave the database: `school_linked_busy` answers the school's owner and managers with the times alone, for a fortnight at most, and row level security keeps the instructor's bookings, payments and learners from the school as before. The diary's instructor and transmission filters include them. With linked instructors, the school's own are headed "Teaching under the school", as on the Instructors screen.
- **Options:** the linked instructor's diary with the learners' first names (their learners are theirs, D-282); free slots worked out from working hours (the times they are taken answer the same question with less to keep right); live updates as on the school's own diary (the school cannot listen to another Business's bookings, and a reload is enough to send a pupil).

## D-285 | 2026-10-20 | Linked instructors on the public pages, and what the tick says | Claude
- **Decision:** a linked instructor's profile says "Works with" and links to the school's page, where a school's own instructor says "Teaches with"; the school's page lists its linked instructors after its own, by the business each runs with their own name above, each opening their own profile, and counts them among its instructors. A school's structured data names only its own instructors as employees, since a linked instructor is self-employed. The platform's tick, and "checked by" the platform on a profile, now follow whether the platform checked the badge itself, read from the database for each instructor on the profile, the school's page and the place pages, rather than guessed from the kind of business: a school vouches for its own instructors and for those linked to it before the platform checked them (D-280), and an instructor checked before linking keeps the tick. Since the platform no longer checks every badge itself, the claims that it does are reworded: the home page says "Badges checked" (the tick is ours, and a driving school checks its own), and the place pages say each instructor has a checked badge.
- **Options:** the same "Teaches with" for both (it suggests the school employs them and sells their lessons, which a learner pays the instructor for); a separate list of linked instructors on the school's page (a learner choosing an instructor does not need to know who sells the lesson before they open the profile); keeping "checked by us" (no longer true of every instructor listed).

## D-286 | 2026-10-20 | A school's recommended and most prices for linked instructors | Claude
- **Decision:** a school whose instructors sell their own lessons has "Prices for linked instructors" in Settings: a recommended price an hour, only ever shown, and the most an hour, both optional, set by whoever sets the school's own prices. The form says the law lets a school recommend a price or set the most, never a fixed or lowest price (VABEO 2022, article 8), and there is no way to set either. The database holds a linked instructor's lesson prices and packages on sale to the most an hour whenever they change one, whatever screen it comes from, and says which school and how much; a price that does not change is left alone, so prices from before the school set its most, or before the instructor linked, stay until they change them, and a package can always be taken off sale. The instructor sees what the school recommends and allows above their prices, with any of theirs above the most listed, and on Your school. Your school also now says the platform checked the badge for an instructor checked before linking, rather than that the school vouches for it.
- **Options:** lowering prices above a new most for the instructor (their prices are theirs, and a learner may be mid booking); refusing bookings at a price above the most (it punishes the learner for the instructor's price); a most per lesson length (an hour's rate covers every length and every package with one number); showing the school which linked instructors charge above it (their prices are public on their profiles already, and nothing here needs it yet).

## D-287 | 2026-10-20 | Fees a linked instructor pays the school, as a record | Claude
- **Decision:** each link carries its terms: a fee every week or every month, the date the arrangement starts, free weeks before the first fee, and a fee for each pupil the school introduces, any of which may be left out (a school may charge for pupils alone). The school's owner and managers set them from Money, Fees, where each linked instructor has a card with the terms, what is owed and every fee. A job inside the database raises each week's or month's fee once, as it begins, at the amount the terms say then, every hour on pg_cron, catching up when it runs late, and raises nothing for a period that begins after the link ended. A fee is owed until the school marks it paid outside the app, saying how if it likes, or writes it off; either can be undone for five seconds, and a fee put back is owed again. The instructor sees the same terms and fees on Your school, without the buttons. A new amount applies from the next fee; a new start, or a change between weekly and monthly, starts a new schedule, which may not begin before today or overlap a fee already raised. Dates in fees leave the year out, as the PRD writes them. Nothing here collects money: that waits for the accountant and the solicitor (M9-14).
- **Free weeks:** the weeks before the first fee, as a new instructor's introductory offer; holiday weeks during the arrangement are not modelled yet, and a school writes off the week instead.
- **Options:** a fee due at the end of its period (franchise fees are paid in advance); free weeks as particular holiday weeks (more to set and explain, and the write-off covers it); letting the instructor mark a fee paid (it is the school's money to confirm).

## D-288 | 2026-10-20 | New pupils for a school: asked on its page, or added by the office | Claude
- **Decision:** a school's public page asks "Ask about lessons": a name, an email or a mobile or both, the postcode lessons would start from, the gearbox (either is fine), when suits, and a box agreeing to the school keeping the details and passing them to one of its instructors. The words agreed to are kept with the enquiry. Anybody may ask, signed in or not; the database counts how often one place asks (ten an hour, two thousand an hour for everybody), a box nobody sees catches scripts, and the answer says nothing about the inbox. The school's owner and managers find them in New pupils (from Learners, and under More), in four parts: new, offered to instructors, taken by one, and closed; one can be closed with why and opened again, and the office can add somebody who rang once they have agreed, in the office's own words. Nobody else reads enquiries, not even the school's instructors. An enquiry closed, taken or left untouched for a year is deleted each night. This is the school's half of the waiting list in GAP-01; an independent instructor's waiting list follows it.
- **Options:** sending the enquiry straight to an instructor (the school decides who gets a pupil, D-278); a confirmation email before the school sees it (the school calls them back anyway, and an unconfirmed lead is still a lead); letting instructors at the school read the inbox (pupils are the school's to give).

## D-289 | 2026-10-20 | Finding a new pupil an instructor: offered to linked ones, or given to the school's own | Claude
- **Decision:** from New pupils the school offers a pupil to one or more of its linked instructors, for four hours, a day, two days or a week. Each sees the offer under Your school, first on the page while it waits: a first name, the postcode district, the gearbox and when suits, never who they are or how to reach them. The first to accept gets the pupil: the database locks the enquiry, so two accepting at once are answered one after the other, and the rest of the offers are withdrawn. Once accepted, the instructor sees the pupil's name, email, mobile and postcode, and the school sees who took them. A decline, or an offer nobody answers in time (checked every ten minutes on pg_cron), puts the pupil back in New if nothing else is open; closing the enquiry, or the instructor leaving the school, withdraws what is open. Or the school gives the pupil to one of its own instructors, with the suggestions it makes for any learner (SCH-03), from where they asked to start and the gearbox they want: the account they will claim is made as for a learner added by hand (D-068), only after the enquiry has been read through the caller's own session and found still waiting, and the database makes them the school's learner with that instructor in the same step that marks the enquiry taken, or the account is removed again. Details that already belong to an account are refused with the advice to send them a link from Learners.
- **Options:** sending the offer to every linked instructor at once (the school chooses who suits); showing the contact before accepting (the pupil agreed to one instructor, not to all of them); a pupil queue the instructors take from themselves (the school decides, D-278).

## D-290 | 2026-10-20 | A pupil a school introduced, and the fee for them | Claude
- **Decision:** a learner relationship says which school introduced the learner (`introduced_by`). A linked instructor who accepted a pupil puts them on their own list from Your school with "Add to my learners": the account they will claim is made from the details the school passed on (D-068), only after the pupil has been read through the instructor's own session and found theirs, and the database links them as the school's introduction, or the account is removed again. The instructor's page for that learner says "Introduced by" the school, read by name through a narrow function, since the school's own row is not theirs to read. A pupil the school gives to one of its own instructors is its introduction too. The school's fee for each pupil it introduces falls due once, today, when the pupil's first lesson with the linked instructor is booked (asked for, held for payment or confirmed), at the fee the link says then, and says the pupil's name on both statements; nothing falls due for the school's own learners. A pupil whose details already belong to an account is sent a link from Learners instead, and is not recorded as introduced: that waits until an invitation can carry it.
- **Options:** the fee when the pupil is accepted (the instructor would pay for somebody who never books); the fee when the first lesson is taken (a lesson cancelled late would be argued over); asking the instructor to record where each learner came from by hand (the system already knows).

## D-291 | 2026-10-20 | What a school pays its own instructors, and the week's statement | Claude
- **Decision:** a school that sells its own lessons says what it pays each instructor teaching under it: a rate for every hour taught, no-shows included, or a share of each lesson's price. Pay is set by the owner or a manager allowed to see revenue, from a Monday on (this week's unless they choose an earlier week in the last year), and every change is kept, so a week already paid always says what it said; the screen shows what applied that week, and what applies now when it differs. Pay is kept apart from memberships, which every manager reads; only the owner, a manager allowed to see revenue and the instructor themselves read it. Instructor pay, under Money and More, shows a week at a time, Monday to Sunday in the UK: each instructor's lessons that have ended and were not called off, with who, how long, the price and the pay, and their total; the same week downloads as a CSV file, a line a lesson and a total each. An instructor teaching under a school sees their own week under More, Your pay, and downloads it too. Nothing is paid from the app: the school pays its instructors itself (D-278).
- **Options:** a monthly statement (franchises and schools run weekly); pay changed for weeks already done (a payslip that changes after it was paid is not a payslip); pay only for completed lessons (a no-show is still the instructor's time, and the school is paid for it).

## D-292 | 2026-10-20 | Joining a school with a business of your own, and leaving | Claude
- **Decision:** following the owner's answer of 20 October (D-278), an instructor who runs their own Business may join a school to teach under it on the same account: their Business pauses (a new standing, `paused`), so its profile and booking link go offline and it opens no portal, and each of its current learners is asked on their home screen whether to move to the school with them, or not now. A Business pauses only with no lessons still to come and no learner holding hours they paid for, as those are owed by it (PAY-12); the invitation says so before they join, and the refusal says what to do. A school it was linked to ends the link as leaving always does. Leaving the school (the school switches them off, D-120), they are sent to open their Business again rather than being given a new one, and learners who stayed with it are its learners still; learners not yet answered have nothing left to choose. When a linked instructor leaves or is let go, the learners the school introduced are asked whether to go back to the school, which finds them an instructor, or to stay. Moving is always the learner's to say: nothing moves until they do, and their record stays where it was made. Somebody who teaches for a Business they do not own still cannot join (D-118). This replaces D-118's answer for owners of a Business of one.
- **Options:** moving every learner with the instructor (their data would go to a school they never chose); closing the Business instead of pausing it (leaving the school would mean starting again); allowing lessons to come or prepaid hours (they would be orphaned, or need refunds this milestone does not build).

## D-293 | 2026-10-20 | The owner invites managers | Claude
- **Decision:** the owner of a school invites managers from the Instructors screen with "Invite a manager", which makes the same one-use link as for an instructor, sent from the owner's own phone (D-118), and says what a manager does: looks after the school's instructors, bookings, prices and learners, and sees its revenue only when the owner allows (D-120). The message reads "would like you to help run it", the invitation waits on the team as one "to help run the school", and the link's page asks the person to help run the school, signed in or out. A manager joins with no instructor profile, as they teach nowhere because of it, and opens the school portal. Only the owner invites a manager; managers and the owner invite instructors as before. This ends D-118's "Inviting managers waits".
- **Options:** managers inviting managers (the owner decides who runs the school); a role choice inside "Invite an instructor" (a manager is not an instructor, and the button would say otherwise).

## D-294 | 2026-10-20 | A school adds and imports its own learners | Claude
- **Decision:** the owner or a manager of a school adds a learner the school already teaches from Learners, as an instructor does (LRN-03, D-068): the app makes the account they will claim, and `add_school_learner` links it to the school, with one of its instructors still teaching there or with nobody yet, to be given one with Assign (SCH-03). A spreadsheet goes in from "Import a spreadsheet" inside the same sheet, every row with nobody yet, in batches under the instructor's rules: somebody already on the school's list with that number or email is refused by name, and details that already belong to an account are sent a link instead. The allowance is the instructor's, 300 an hour for each person adding. Instructors at the school add their own learners as before, and cannot add the school's.
- **Options:** a column for the instructor in the spreadsheet (most schools' files have none, and a misspelt name would fail the row); every learner the school adds going to an instructor at once (the office often takes a pupil before knowing who has room).

## D-295 | 2026-10-20 | What a school's arrangements tell people | Claude
- **Decision:** seven notices join Appendix B's list for M9. A linked instructor hears when a school offers them a pupil (a first name, the postcode district and the gearbox, as the offer shows before accepting, and when to answer by), when the school sets or changes their fees, when each fee falls due, and when one is marked paid or written off. The school's owner and managers hear when a linked instructor takes a pupil. A learner hears when they are asked whether to move, in the words of the choice on their home screen. When a link ends, the side that did not end it hears; an instructor the school switches off hears, and is told to open their own Business again when it was paused for the school. Offers, fees, moves and partings are service messages, so they always reach the inbox; a pupil taken is not, so a school can switch it off. They sit in the existing groups (Your account, Money), so the settings screen does not change. Triggers queue the events, so no RPC was reopened, and the reader answers nothing for a change that has moved on, such as an offer taken before the job ran. A manager switched off is not told by this notice; the owner tells them.
- **Options:** a new settings group for schools (a fifth switch for a handful of notices most people never get); queuing from each RPC (eight functions reopened, and the hourly fee job and a pupil's first lesson raise fees too); telling a school when an offer lapses with nobody taking it (the pupil is back in New, which is where the school looks).

## D-296 | 2026-10-20 | The privacy notice and both sets of terms cover a school's arrangements | Claude
- **Decision:** the privacy notice says what is held when somebody asks a school for lessons, and what an instructor who works with the school sees before and after taking them on; what is held about instructors who work with a school, what a school pays its own instructors, and learners asked whether to move; why a pupil's details pass from a school to an instructor (they asked and agreed, in words kept with the request); how long each is kept, an enquiry a year after it is closed, taken or last touched; and what a school sees of an instructor who works with it. The terms for instructors and schools gain "Working with a school": prices within the most the school sets, fees recorded and not collected, pupils offered, either side stopping, pausing a Business to join one, and pay records. They also say a school vouches for its instructors' badges (D-280), who the controller is once a pupil is passed on, and that the one time we ask an instructor's learners about moving is the instructor's own doing. The learners' terms gain "If you learn through a school". All three are dated 20 October 2026, which asks everybody to agree again at their next sign in (D-265), as a change of this size should. Every new question is left for the solicitor beside it.
- **Options:** keeping the old dates (people would be held to words they never saw, which D-265 rules out); a separate agreement between a school and the instructors it works with (the solicitor is asked whether one is needed).

## D-297 | 2026-10-20 | "New learner invite accepted" is sent, withdrawn for D-301 | Claude
- **Decision:** M9-31 sent Appendix B's "New learner invite accepted" from a trigger on a learner invitation being stamped accepted. Merging `beta-prep` into M9 for one release (the product owner's choice, 20 October) found that `beta-prep` had already built the same notice as D-301, with a migration of the same name, so M9's copy was taken out before anything reached `main` and D-301 is the one that ships.
- **Options:** keeping both (every learner joining would be told twice, and both define `system_learner_joined_notice`); keeping M9's instead (D-301 was built and reviewed on the branch the owner releases from).

## D-300 | 2026-10-20 | The cancellation policy is said on the booking link and the pay screen | Claude
- **Found:** checking the launch posts against the code. The page for learners says "Their cancellation policy is shown before you book", and nothing showed it: the booking link said nothing about cancelling, and the pay screen named "its cancellation policy" without saying what it was.
- **Decision:** both now say it in one sentence made from the Business's rules (R-06): "You can cancel for free up to 48 hours before the lesson. After that, cancelling or missing it costs the full price." Half the price for a fee of 50%, "until the lesson starts" where there is no window, and "You can cancel for free at any time." where there is no fee. Missing a lesson is in the same sentence because it costs what cancelling late does (R-09). On the booking link it sits just above the Book button; on the pay screen it sits under the price for a lesson still to be paid for, asked for or charged the day before, and not for a fee or a lesson already paid. The words are `cancellationPolicyWords` in `packages/core/src/cancellation.ts`, tested against what `cancellationOutcome` and `noShowOutcome` then do, so the sentence cannot promise one thing while the cancellation does another.
- **Data:** somebody on a booking link usually has no account, and the settings that make up the policy are not theirs to read. `cancellation_policy(p_instructor_id)` answers the two numbers and nothing else, for an instructor whose link takes bookings, from `private.booking_rules`, which `cancel_booking` and `mark_no_show` read too. The pay screen asks the same function. It is allowlisted for visitors in the security test, with its reason. It is a function of its own rather than two more fields on `booking_page`, because the schools milestone is changing `booking_page` on its own branch at the same time, and two migrations replacing one function leave whichever runs second without the other's change.
- **Deploy:** either order is safe. Until the function exists the page cannot read the policy, and shows everything else without the line.
- **Options:** change the line on the learners page instead (the policy is what a learner most needs to know before paying, BOK-09); two more fields on `booking_page` (collides with the schools branch); a function of its own (chosen). The package page's save-card note also names the policy without saying it, and is left for now: the claim is about booking.

## D-301 | 2026-10-20 | An instructor is told when a learner they invited joins | Claude
- **Found:** checking the launch posts against the code. The notice "{Name} joined you" was in the catalogue with its words, and nothing sent it, so an instructor found out that a learner had accepted their invitation only by looking at their list. Appendix B has the row: "New learner invite accepted", to the instructor and the school, by push.
- **Decision:** accepting an invitation queues `learner.joined` with the invitation's id, in the same transaction that links the learner, whichever way it is accepted: the Accept button for somebody already signed in, or the sign-up an invitation link started. A job then tells the instructor the learner is now with ("Priya Patel joined you. They accepted your invitation and can be booked in now.", opening the learner's card) and, at a school, its owners and managers ("Priya Patel joined. Ian One invited them. They can be booked in now.", opening the school's learners), who are the people a school hears through about its instructors (D-260). In the inbox and by push, as the catalogue already had it, in "Your account" where push can be switched off; once for each invitation, however often the event arrives.
- **Who it names:** the learner by the name they gave when they signed up, or the one on the invitation where they gave none.
- **Options:** tell whoever made the invitation (at a school that can be a manager inviting on an instructor's behalf, who hears as one of its managers anyway); tell the instructor the learner is with, and the school (chosen, as Appendix B has it).

## D-302 | 2026-10-20 | Charging the saved card the day before is Pro only | Product owner, Claude
- **Found:** checking the launch posts against the code. The pricing page sells "Charge the saved card before each lesson" as part of Pro, from `autoChargeBeforeLesson` in `packages/config/src/plans.ts`; PRD 9.18 lists "auto-charge before lesson" under Pro, and D-022 says the Pro features in Phase 1, auto-charge among them, still need an entitlement check. Nothing checks it: Payment setup offers "The day before the lesson" on every plan, `set_payment_mode` takes it from any owner, and the day-before charge runs whatever the plan.
- **What it costs today:** little. Every new Business starts on Pro or School (a founding place, or a trial once those have gone), so for now a Business is on Free only if staff put it there or a paid plan ends.
- **Options:**
  1. **Gate it, as PRD 9.18 and D-022 say (recommended).** On Free, Payment setup shows "The day before the lesson" as a Pro choice with the way to upgrade, and `set_payment_mode` refuses it with `PLAN_REQUIRED`, as `set_brand_colour` does for colours (D-210), with a pgTAP test. To settle with it: a Business that drops to Free with it chosen keeps the setting, and its new lessons are paid at booking until it is on Pro again; lessons already booked to be charged the day before are still charged, because those are the terms they were booked on (PAY-03), which the screen already promises.
  2. **Take it off the Pro list.** Every plan keeps charging the day before, and the pricing page, the plan screen and PRD 9.18 stop selling it as Pro. A small change, but Pro loses one of the things it is sold on.
- **Asked:** on 20 October 2026, with the report on these fixes.
- **Answered:** the product owner, the same day: "D-302: gate it, option 1. Charging the saved card the day before is Pro only, as the PRD and pricing page say."
- **Done, as option 1:** `set_payment_mode` refuses `before_lesson` on Free with `PLAN_REQUIRED`. `private.payment_mode`, which every booking function reads, answers `at_booking` for a Business on Free that chose it, so its new lessons are paid at booking (a learner's held while they pay, R-10) while the choice stays saved, and it works again the moment the Business is back on Pro. Lessons already booked to be charged the day before still are: the charge reads each lesson's own terms, never the plan. A lesson whose length is changed starts its money again (D-188) and so takes the terms of the day. Payment setup on Free lists "The day before the lesson (Pro)", which cannot be picked, with the Pro tag and "See what Pro includes"; a choice kept from Pro says "Until you are on Pro again, learners pay when they book." Only the owner sees this, since only the owner chooses and the plan is theirs to know (D-123).

## D-303 | 2026-10-20 | The cancellation policy is said on the package page too | Claude
- **Found:** left out of D-300. The package page told a learner who saves a card that the Business "can charge it a late cancellation or no-show fee under its cancellation policy", and did not say what the policy was.
- **Decision:** the page says it in the words the booking link and the pay screen use, from `cancellationPolicyWords`, as the last line of the list of what the credit is for: "You can cancel for free up to 48 hours before the lesson. After that, cancelling or missing it costs the full price." Shown while the package can be bought, as the pay screen shows it only for a lesson still to be paid for. The save-card note is unchanged, and the policy it names is now on the same card.
- **Where:** in the list rather than just above the buttons, where the booking link has it, because the first thing above the buttons here is the 14 days to change your mind about the package, and two kinds of cancelling side by side read as one.
- **Data:** a package belongs to a Business, not to an instructor, so `cancellation_policy(p_instructor_id)` does not fit. The window and the fee are the Business's to set (R-06), with nothing for an instructor, so the policy is the Business's settings over the platform's defaults: the merge `private.booking_rules` makes for `cancel_booking` and `mark_no_show`. A learner may read both already, under row-level security: the Business they learn with through `businesses_select_linked_learner` (the package is visible to them on the same terms), and the platform's defaults, which anybody signed in reads. `businessCancellationPolicy` in `apps/web/src/lib/booking/rules.ts` merges them with `resolveBookingRules`, as the warning before cancelling a lesson does, so no new function and no migration. When either cannot be read the line is left out, rather than the platform's defaults said as if they were the Business's.
- **Options:** a function like `cancellation_policy` that takes a Business (a migration and a pgTAP file, to answer what the learner can read already); the line just above the buttons, as on the booking link; inside the save-card note (seen only by somebody paying with a new card); the list (chosen).
- **Left:** for a Business whose fee is 0%, the save-card notes here and on the pay screen still say it "can charge it a late cancellation or no-show fee", next to a policy that says cancelling is free at any time. Nothing is charged; the note promises more than will happen. For the product owner to say whether those notes should drop the fee when there is none.

## D-304 | 2026-10-20 | A portal's top bar keeps its size at 200% text, and the sweep sees a phone zoom out | Claude
- **Found:** checking the package page at 390 px with text at 200% (D-303). A portal's top bar on a phone (the logo, support and the bell) was sized in rem, so it doubled with the text, and the bell sat 87 px past the edge of the screen on every learner, instructor and school screen. The sweep passed. A phone shows a page that is too wide by zooming out until all of it fits, so the window grows to the page's width, and the sweep measured against the window.
- **Decision:** the bar's spacing, the logo in it and the two buttons are in pixels (a 56 px bar, a 32 px logo, 48 px buttons), so they keep their size at any text size. None of them is text, and the icons in the buttons were 24 px already; a touch target need not grow with the text. The count on the bell is text, and still grows. The sweep now adds how far the window has grown past the screen the test is on to what reaches past the window, which is D-219's measure and unchanged. With the old bar, all 22 phone portal screens in the sweep fail by 87 px; with the new one, the whole sweep passes at both widths.
- **Also found behind it:** the instructor's home ran 9 px past a phone's edge at 200% text. In Upcoming lessons the date column took 192 px and left the learner, the lesson and the pickup a sliver, and a pickup of one word ("Home") was wider than that. The column now starts at 6rem, and takes a line of its own when there is no room for that; at normal text the row is unchanged. The nine pixels D-219 put down to a scrollbar may have been this row: on a phone the window grows past the screen only when something is too wide, and the row comes and goes with the seeded lessons.
- **Trade-off:** at 200% text the count covers most of the bell, as it partly did before. The number stays readable, and the link is named "Notifications, 3 unread".
- **Options:** a bar that wraps onto two lines; the wheel alone instead of the whole logo at large text (CSS cannot tell large text from a wide screen short of container queries); pixels for what is not text (chosen).

## D-305 | 2026-10-20 | What a deep test of the school side found, and fixed | Claude
- **Asked for:** the product owner, after M9 went live: sign up, invite instructors, and go through every flow, linked and unlinked, leaving, learners, schools and the notices, to spot any error.
- **How:** on a copy of what is live, with its own database, so nobody real was signed up, emailed or texted. New people went through the screens a person would, at phone width: a school owner signing up with two-step verification, an instructor joining by the school's link, an instructor with a business of their own linking it, a learner accepting an invitation, somebody asking the school for lessons, offers taken and left open, fees set, due and paid, prices held to the school's most, a pupil given to the school's own instructor and her pay, a manager joining, learners added and imported, the linked instructor leaving, joining with his own business and being switched off, and every notice in the feeds. Each step was photographed, and every browser error and failed request recorded: there were none.
- **Fixed:**
  1. On a phone the cookie question covered the last button on a page until it was answered, and the page could not be scrolled clear of it: a new learner could not press Finish on About you. While it is asked, the page has its height to spare at the end, and the browser keeps what it scrolls to clear of it (WCAG 2.4.11). It still sits over the page rather than pushing it down (D-152).
  2. A learner who moved with their instructor stayed a learner of the Business they moved from too, so an instructor who opened their own Business again found them on it, though its page says it comes back with the learners who stayed. Moving now leaves that Business, as an instructor marking somebody as left does, and their lessons and records stay where they were made. Anybody who moved before is put right by the migration.
  3. A linked instructor's first price above the school's most said only "Check the highlighted fields", with nothing highlighted. It now says the most the school allows, in the words of the price table (D-286), in Settings and in onboarding.
  4. An instructor teaching under a school was asked on Today to connect payments, which only the school's owner can do, so their list could never finish. It is not on their list.
  5. An instructor joining a school was asked for "Your name and your business" with no business to name. It asks for their name.
- **Seen and as designed:** an offer accepted, or a fee paid, before the sweep sends its notice is not announced (D-295); after leaving a school, an unchecked badge goes back to being unchecked and the plan to what it was.
- **Left for the owner:** the pages for schools and pricing describe a school that sells its own lessons; nothing yet says a school can link instructors who keep their own business, charge them fees, take new pupils from its page or pay its own instructors.
- **Options:** pushing the page up by the question's height instead (it moves what somebody is reading, which D-152 rules out); leaving a learner who moved on both lists (two Businesses would think they teach them).

## D-306 | 2026-10-21 | The school is told when a learner moves to it | Product owner, Claude
- **Found:** in the second deep test of the school side. A learner who chose to move to a school, following an instructor who joined it or going back after a linked instructor left, appeared on its list and nobody was told. One who came back showed "Nobody yet" until somebody in the office happened to look. The product owner asked for this and the test's other three suggestions on 21 October.
- **Decision:** answering yes queues `learner.moved` with the move, as asking queued `learner.move_asked` (D-295). The school's owners and managers hear "Pat Jones came back to you. Ian Driving no longer works with you, and they chose to come back. Choose who teaches them." or "Lara Smith moved to you. They came with Ian One, who keeps teaching them.", opening the school's learners. The instructor they came with hears "Lara Smith moved with you", opening their card. In the inbox, by push and by email, under "Your account", and always in the inbox, because a learner nobody teaches is waiting on the school. A learner who stays, or a question withdrawn when an instructor opens their own Business again, is nobody's news.
- **With it:** the "Stop linking" box, which said what happens to the instructor's business, badge and Pro, now also says that no new fees are raised for them and that any pupils the school introduced to them are asked whether to come back, the start of the same story.
- **Not in the PRD:** Appendix B has no row for it. It follows "New learner invite accepted" (D-301), which tells the same people.
- **Options:** the school only (the instructor they came with knew they were asked, not what they answered); the inbox and push only, as a learner joining is (an office at a desk reads its email, and a learner with nobody teaching them needs an answer, as a pupil offered does).

## D-307 | 2026-10-21 | A profile with no base says nothing about miles | Claude
- **Found:** in the second deep test of the school side. An instructor who skipped "Where do you teach?", as a school's instructors often do, had a public profile with a drawn circle saying "8 miles around" and "Lessons within 8 miles of their base". There was no base: 8 miles is only the setting's starting value.
- **Decision:** with no base, "Where lessons start" draws no circle and gives no distance. It says the districts the instructor added ("Lessons in LS17 and LS18."), or, with none, the section is left out. A base whose place could not be found still draws the circle around its district, as before. The instructor's own coverage screen is unchanged, because there the circle is the setting they are choosing.
- **Options:** the school's base in place of theirs (a school's instructors cover different places, and it would be a claim about somebody else's area); a line asking the learner to get in touch (it says nothing the booking page does not).

## D-308 | 2026-10-21 | The pages for schools and pricing describe what M9 built | Claude
- **Found:** D-305 left for the owner that the pages for schools and pricing described a school that sells its own lessons, and said nothing of linked instructors, their fees, new pupils from a school's page or a school's pay for its instructors. The product owner asked for it on 21 October.
- **Decision:** the schools page says who sells the lessons in place of "Payments to the school", adds learners from a spreadsheet, instructor pay, badges the school vouches for and being told as things happen, and gains a band, "Instructors with their own business": they keep their business, prices the school recommends with a most, fees on the school's terms, new pupils from its page, Pro while linked, and moving being the learner's choice. Its plan band says "One plan for the whole school", with linked instructors counted. The School plan's card, on both pages, adds linked instructors and their fees, new pupils from the school's page and weekly pay statements: what a plan has that is no entitlement of its own. Pricing answers how the School plan is priced and whether a school can work with instructors who run their own business. Every figure still comes from `@repo/config/plans`, and nothing not built is sold as there (D-116): collecting a fee by card (M9-14) is not mentioned.
- **Options:** a page of its own for linked instructors (one more page to keep true, for a feature schools read about on this one).

## D-309 | 2026-10-21 | vite is raised to 8.3.4 under vitest, past an advisory that turned CI red | Claude
- **Found:** CI on `m9-schools` at 4795e2b passed every job but "Dependency advisories (high and above)", on a push with no dependency change: GHSA-587h-7wmj-h5pm, high, had been published against vite 8.3.0 to 8.3.2. The check vite's dev server makes before opening a file in an editor can be got round on Windows. Fixed in 8.3.3.
- **Decision:** `vite` is overridden to `>=8.3.3 <8.4`, which resolves to 8.3.4, out for 13 days. It reaches us only under vitest, which runs our own tests on a developer's machine and in CI; the app is built and served by Next, so nothing a visitor reaches runs vite. Every package's unit tests pass on it.
- **Options:** adding it to `ignoreGhsas` (a fixed version exists, which is what separates an override from an ignore, D-240); an open `>=8.3.3` (it would let vite's next minor in with the fix, and a test tool's minor is a change to read).

## D-310 | 2026-10-21 | A fee is said to come off a saved card only when there is one | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. After a no-show or a late cancellation, a learner who had once paid by card was told "the £21 fee is being charged to your saved card". With no card kept, nothing was charged, the fee stayed unpaid, and nobody told them to pay it.
- **Decision:** before a notice about a fee says it is being charged, the job asks the payments provider whether the learner has a card kept with that Business that has not run out. With none, the notice says the fee is owed, and the learner's money screen offers to pay it, as for any fee. A lesson paid by card is charged on that card, as before. The provider being unreachable fails the job, which tries again, rather than guessing either way.
- **Options:** keeping cards in our own database (the provider is the only record of them, D-198); saying nothing about how the fee is paid (the learner needs to know whether to act).

## D-311 | 2026-10-21 | A paused Business takes no bookings | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. An instructor who joined a school paused their own Business, as the joining page promises, but its booking link still took bookings.
- **Decision:** a paused Business is treated as a suspended one for booking: its link says "This link is not taking bookings", and every way of booking refuses it with `BUSINESS_SUSPENDED`. Its profile was already offline.
- **Options:** a message of its own on the link (the learner can do nothing with the reason, and the instructor's school page is where to find them now).

## D-312 | 2026-10-21 | An instructor whose own Business is paused can be switched back on | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. A school could not switch a member back on while their own Business was paused: "They teach for another driving business now". The other Business was theirs, paused to teach at the school.
- **Decision:** switching back on looks past a Business of the member's own that is paused. One they still run, or another school, still refuses it.

## D-313 | 2026-10-21 | Fees still owed when a link ends stay on both Fees screens | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. When a link ended, the fees still owed under it disappeared from the school's Fees screen and from the instructor's school page, while the school's Money screen still said "£45 owed". Nobody could mark them paid.
- **Decision:** an ended link with fees still owed stays on the school's Fees screen, after the linked instructors, as "Sam Driving, no longer linked since Wed 21 Oct", with no terms to change: "No new fees are raised. Mark what is still owed paid, or write it off." The instructor's school page shows "Still owed to Explore School", with each fee. Once everything is settled the card goes. `links_still_owing` answers each side for its own links only.

## D-314 | 2026-10-21 | Signing in from a school's invitation goes back to the invitation | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. "Sign in" on a school's "teach with us" invitation dropped it without a word: the instructor landed on Today, with their own Business untouched. Had nothing been in the way, it would have joined them and paused their Business with no warning.
- **Decision:** only an account made to join, with nothing of its own yet, joins as it signs in (AUTH-05, D-118). Anybody who already teaches, learns or has a Business of their own, paused or not, is taken back to the invitation once signed in. It says what joining does, or why it cannot yet, and they join with their own yes.
- **Options:** a `next` on the sign-in link (a learner's invitation is used as they sign in, so the page it went back to would say the link no longer works).

## D-315 | 2026-10-21 | A booked lesson makes an enquiry or a waiting learner active | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. An invited learner starts as an enquiry and nothing moved them on: with lessons booked and paid for they still read "Enquiry", never counted towards Free's ten (D-208) and never showed under Active.
- **Decision:** once a lesson with them is confirmed, however it was booked, an enquiry or somebody waiting is active, written down as "Moved from enquiry to active when a lesson was booked". A trigger does it, for the reason the Free limit is one. On Free with no room left an enquiry stays an enquiry and the lesson stands: a lesson, or a payment for one, is never refused over a count. Somebody who has passed or left is not moved back. Everybody already booked was moved on the same way, oldest first.
- **Options:** refusing the booking at the limit (a payment confirming a lesson could then fail); moving them when a request is made (a request may be declined).

## D-316 | 2026-10-21 | A lesson started early has its record | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. A lesson's card offers to start it a quarter of an hour early (D-178), but its record was refused until the minute it was due, as "Too close to another lesson".
- **Decision:** a record is taken from the same quarter of an hour before the start. One for a lesson further off is refused as `LESSON_NOT_STARTED`: "This lesson has not started yet. Save its record once it has."

## D-317 | 2026-10-21 | A switched-off instructor's learners are plain to see | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. Switching an instructor off keeps their learners with them, and nothing said so: the school's list went on saying "With Sam", and nothing prompted anybody to give them to somebody else.
- **Decision:** the switch-off box says "Their 3 learners stay with them until you give each one to another instructor on Learners.", beside what it already says about lessons to come. The school's learner list says "With Sam, who is switched off" until the learner is given to somebody else. The team carries how many learners each member teaches.
- **Options:** giving them to nobody as the instructor is switched off (switching back on would then lose who taught whom).

## D-318 | 2026-10-21 | The booking confirmation says what is left to do | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. After booking on a link, the confirmation said "Pay for it now" when credit had paid for the lesson and when it was only a request, and "Lesson booked" while the time was only held until it was paid for.
- **Decision:** what is left to do is read from the lesson as it now is. Paid from credit: "Paid from your lesson credit." and no button to pay. Held while it is paid for: "Lesson held", "It is yours until 14:47: pay by then to confirm it." and "Pay to confirm it". A request at a Business that takes cards: "Authorise the payment", with "Your card is only charged if they accept." A lesson to pay for later keeps "Pay for it now". With instant booking off, the button says "Ask for 12:00", not "Book 12:00 for £42".

## D-319 | 2026-10-21 | A day picked before the booking link is ready counts | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. A day picked before the page had finished loading was ignored: the box showed the new day while the times were another day's.
- **Decision:** the box is read once the page is ready, and the times follow what it shows. "Times on Tue 27 Oct" is now said above the times as well as announced, so it is plain which day they are for.

## D-320 | 2026-10-21 | Why a school cannot sell every lesson while instructors are linked | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. Choosing "We sell the lessons" while instructors were linked said only "Check the highlighted fields", with nothing highlighted.
- **Decision:** the choice says "Some instructors linked to the school sell their own lessons. Choose Some of each while they are linked."

## D-321 | 2026-10-21 | A franchise school sees the instructors it asked to link | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. "Invited so far" in a school's setup listed invitations to join only, so a school whose instructors sell their own lessons saw nothing there.
- **Decision:** it lists invitations to link a business too: "Link to link their business works until Wed 4 Nov", then "Has linked their business" with a "Linked" pill once used.

## D-322 | 2026-10-21 | Learners who have never signed in can be answered for | Claude
- **Found:** in the third deep test. Joining a school asks each of the instructor's learners whether to move with them. A learner added by hand or imported has an account they have never claimed (D-068), so could never answer, and nobody was told: ten of the twelve.
- **Decision:** the instructor's Learners screen shows "Asked to move with you": each learner their own Business asked, how each answered, and how many have still to. For a learner who has never signed in, "Answer for them" asks the instructor to ask them, then say what they chose ("Only say they are moving once they have agreed"), as the office does for somebody who agreed on the phone (D-288). The answer moves them exactly as the learner's own would, and the record says the instructor gave it. A learner who has signed in answers for themselves.
- **Options:** moving them without asking (moving is the learner's choice, D-292); leaving them with the paused Business and nobody told (what happened).

## D-323 | 2026-10-21 | The third deep test's wording | Claude
- **Found and decided:**
  - A link invitation said the school "vouches for your badge" to an instructor whose badge the platform had already checked. Signed in, it now says which is true; signed out, "vouches for your badge, unless Doovor has checked it already". Pro while linked is a line of its own.
